- **2026-11-04 (8): idle time re-sampled after the GPU selection; Nsight Compute blocked.** `nvidia-smi dmon` over a 15k prompt (467.3 tok/s that run): a kernel running in 84% of the seconds (72% before), PCIe rx averaging 9.8 GB/s (5.9 before) with 18-35 GB/s bursts now at 84-100% SM - copy and compute overlap more, the GPU idles ~16% (part of it the run's ramp seconds). **Tensor-core attention is not started: the kernel is not shown to be bandwidth- or tensor-bound.** `attention_group_sel_rows` keeps scores and exponentials in a double scratch in global memory and accumulates in double (reference-exact), ~512 selected tokens a row; a mma kernel is inexact (a fast-mode kernel) and a guess without a profile. `ncu` (Nsight Compute 2026.3.1 is installed) refused: ERR_NVGPUCTRPERM - GPU performance counters need an admin or the NVIDIA Control Panel's 'Allow access to the GPU performance counters to all users' (a system setting, left to the user). The first attempt also left one orphan `shadow.exe` (16 GB, pid 5680) running after `ncu` was refused; killed.

- **2026-11-04 (9): Nsight Compute on `attention_group_sel_rows` (elevated, `bench/ncu_attn_group.ncu-repz`, `bench/ncu_attn_group.ps1`): FP64-bound - and a float version is a tie.** Per launch (32 rows, grid 6 x 32 blocks of 256): 932 us, **FP64 pipe 74% busy**, DRAM 2.3%, L2 31%, L1 hit 35%, 80 registers, 33.8 KB shared a block -> theoretical occupancy 33% (achieved 31%), 12.9 cycles a warp between issues, issue slots 29% busy. So tensor cores are the wrong tool (it is not bandwidth- or tensor-bound; the scores, exponentials and the weighted sum run in double on a card with FP64 at 1/64 of FP32). `attention_group_sel_rows_f32` (same kernel, scores/softmax/weighted sum in float): prompt tok/s float / double, three interleaved pairs, 9.5k 412.4, 431.0, 431.8 / 424.7, 428.6, 429.5, 15k 443.2, 428.2, 452.7 / 434.9, 440.9, 445.3: total 170.3 s / 170.0 s = **a tie** (the double kernel's ~1 s a pass was not on the critical path). Reply agreement with `--exact` on three ~9k prompts (120 tokens): float 0.046, 0.063, 0.018, double 0.026, 0.063, 0.138 (both low: the fast modes diverge early on long prompts). `SHADOW_ATT_F32=1` turns the float kernel on; default is the double. Taken together with the copy-lead and float-index-score ties: **the pass has several limits within a few percent of each other, so removing one moves nothing** - next is a timeline (Nsight Systems) of what the GPU does between and during layers.

- **2026-11-04 (10): the timeline (Nsight Systems, `bench/nsys-pass36-2026-11-04.nsys-rep`, `tools/nsys_pass_summary.py`) - the pass is paced by the per-layer expert copies at ~14 GB/s.** 9.5k prompt, default build, 36 GiB tier (405.8 tok/s under the tracer), window = first to last sparse-attention launch, 11.29 s: **kernels running 51.3% of it, host-to-device copies 62.9%, both 27.1%, neither 12.9%, copy with no kernel 35.8%**; 96.7 GB copied, in 92 copies of ~1.0 GB (one per layer, 59-72 ms each, **13.3-15.3 GB/s each, the same whether a kernel overlaps (15.3) or none does (13.9)**) plus ~5,900 small ones; one copy in flight at a time; consecutive layers' copies start ~77 ms apart. That is why every compute-side change tied (float index scores, float attention, copy a layer ahead, IQ4_NL tensor cores): the copies set the pace and the kernels (5.8 s of 11.3) fit inside. Kernel time in the window: `attention_group_sel_rows` 23.8%, `attention_heads_rows` 13.2% (the dense path for a pass's first positions, 2,827 launches), `index_scores_rows` 7.6%, `matvec_cols_iq4_nl` 6.6% (37,536 launches of 10 us), the expert kernels ~25%. **Why 14 GB/s:** the link does 28 (PCIe 4.0 x16, STATUS 2026-11-03) and a kernel running does not slow it; the host's DRAM does: `SHADOW_STAGE_NOREAD=1` (timing only, the file-only experts are zeros instead of read: wrong output) gives 9.5k 460.3, 462.3 / 430.7, 430.5 and 15k 488.3, 488.4 / 446.6, 448.8 = **+7% / +9%** from taking the 7.5% of experts that are in no RAM tier out of the copy path - so the file reads cost that much and the rest of the gap (the copies still at ~15 GB/s) is not found; this PC's DRAM is 4 x 16 GB Corsair at a configured 2,933 MT/s (a single thread copies memory at 13.4 GB/s here, 26.8 GB/s of DRAM traffic). Options by size of the bound: the file-only experts without a CPU copy (registered file views, <= +7-9%), fewer bytes a token (rows a pass, VRAM-bound), faster DRAM (BIOS, the owner's call), then compute.

- **2026-11-04 (11): decode profiled (Nsight Systems, `bench/nsys-decode-2026-11-04.nsys-rep`), the router bias task-checked, and CPU experts / pre-gating not re-run.** 4 prompts x 160 tokens (52.4 tok/s under the tracer): the 14.8 s trace has **kernels running 40.3% of the time, host-to-device copies 31.5% (only 4.7% of the time with a kernel running at once), neither 32.8%**; 107.5 GB over 51,648 copies of ~2.1 MB (one expert each, ~23 GB/s a copy, 168 MB a decoded token), ~470 kernels and 23 ms of wall a token (9.3 ms of kernel time). Each layer is a chain - attention / DeltaNet kernels, router logits to the host, the misses copied, then the experts - so the copy engine and the kernels take turns; the gaps between kernels: 3,370 ms in 1,167 gaps over 1 ms, 3,768 ms in 12,987 gaps of 0.1-1 ms. Kernel time: `matvec_cols_q6_k` 18% (13,045 launches of 82 us), the grouped expert matvecs ~45%, `matvec_cols_q8_0` 9%, `delta_step` 6%. This is the 2026-10-27 / 2026-11-04 picture again (demand copies are ~32% of a verify pass), now with the timeline: the earlier tries on it all lost on this PC - CPU experts (one CPU expert ~0.2 ms against ~0.09 ms for the copy), pre-gated prefetch (-66%), zero-copy reads (7 GB/s) - and FreeToken's q* needs host expert bandwidth close to the link's (here ~8-11 GB/s against 22-28). **Router bias, task-checked** (`tools/task_check.py`, 48 auto-graded short tasks: arithmetic, word problems, string reversal, sorting, vowel counts, facts), correct / decode tok/s on `dec.py`: bias 0 **42/48**, 55.8; **0.5: 40/48, 57.2 (+2.5%)**; 1: 40/48, 54.7; `--exact` 37/48 (the string and counting tasks are noisy: the model misses them in every mode). Fewer correct by 2 of 48 is within that noise and +2.5% decode is within launch-to-launch noise (+-3 tok/s): a tie either way, so **it stays opt-in**.

- **2026-11-04 (12): file-only experts copied straight from file views (`SHADOW_FILE_VIEWS=1`, `fileview.rs`): correct, but -58% - off.** A long prompt's experts that are in neither the VRAM cache nor the RAM tier are copied to the stage halves from read-only `MapViewOfFile` views of the model file, registered with `cuMemHostRegister(READ_ONLY | DEVICEMAP)` a layer at a time, instead of being read into pinned buffers by the read pool (no CPU copy through DRAM). Same reply as the CPU-copy path (default-mode hash f35749c5 both ways). Prompt tok/s, views / CPU copy: 9.5k **183.4 / 438.7**, 15k **176.8 / 442.4**. Per layer on the host (`SHADOW_STAGE_TIME`): mapping + registering 8-15 ms for 250-540 pieces (200-350 merged ranges), but **unregistering + unmapping 46-87 ms** (`cuMemHostUnregister` and `UnmapViewOfFile` cost per range), done before the next copy is queued: ~4 s of a ~7 s pass. Two things found on the way: (1) the first version kept one view of the whole file and the pages it touched stayed in the working set (51 GiB peak, the RAM watchdog killed the run at 1.9 GiB available, nothing left behind) - hence per-layer views; (2) the file-only set is bigger than the 7.5% average: **115-180 experts a layer in the late layers** (45% of their 512), 74-95 in the first - the placement profile fills the tier by global heat, not per layer. Not pursued: the registration lifetime would have to be taken off the critical path (a thread that unregisters, or ranges kept registered across passes: the file-only experts of all layers are ~4-6 GiB of pinned page cache, over the RAM floor's room), against a bound of +7-9% (`SHADOW_STAGE_NOREAD`).

- **2026-11-04 (13): the rest of the list - more rows with the drafter's VRAM, the dense attention path, the cache policy.** (2) **Rows with the drafter's 1.2 GB free** (`--dflash off --spec 0`, cap 4,096 / 8,192, two launches each): 9.5k 451.1, 444.1 / 462.1, 464.1 (+3%), 15k 449.3, 445.1 / 437.4, 440.3 (-2%): the copy-paced pass gains nothing from rows past ~4k, so unloading the drafter during a long prompt (deferred observe, weights re-read afterwards) is not built. (3) **`attention_heads_rows`** (13% of the prompt's kernel time, the dense path for a pass's first ~2k positions): not touched - its sparse sibling's float version tied (kernel time off the critical path), and this one is the same kind of kernel. (6) **Cache policy**: the VRAM cache is already pure per-layer LRU (2026-10-28: +11% over pinning, hit rate 59 -> 71-75%, simulated first with `tools/cache_sim.py`); a global cross-layer LRU was not simulated (the routing log is written by the session's Drop and `shadow serve` does not reach it on `/shutdown`: no `SHADOW_ROUTE_LOG` file came out); CPU experts (0.2 ms an expert against 0.09 ms for the copy), pre-gating (-66%) and zero-copy reads (7 GB/s) were measured and lost earlier on this PC.

- **2026-11-04 (14): the pivot - prompt path 2 (`docs/PREFILL2_SPEC.md`); first measurements for it.** Real 6,400-row passes (drafter off; rows used 4,608 and 6,400): 9.5k 461.5, 449.9 vs 452.5, 472.1 at 4,096, 15k 441.2, 437.1 vs 450.0, 448.6: **bigger chunks alone no longer help** (my 2026-11-04 (13) test had only reached ~4.6k rows). Timeline at 6,400 rows: 70.2 GB copied (vs 96.7), kernels busy 54.9%, copies 43.6%, neither 22.9%, **124 gaps of 10-50 ms (3.4 s) + 4 over 50 ms (0.7 s) with no kernel running**. The host chain per layer: router logits down, `route()` for every token (f64 exp over 512 logits each, one thread: ~27 ms at 6,400 rows), bookkeeping, `grouped_plan`, upload, then the experts launch; the GPU waits for all of it and the host cannot queue the next layer first. **Routing spread over the CPU threads** (`route_all`, bit for bit the sequential one; `--exact` hash 162a8e07 unchanged): 9.5k 442.8, 459.2, 458.6 / 15k 456.6, 461.8, 461.3 against 430.7, 430.5, 429.1 / 446.6, 448.8, 442.4 before: +3-5%. With it, after the logits arrive: conversion 2.3 ms, route + bookkeeping + plan prep 3.5-4.6 ms, `moe_staged` 6-27 ms (it includes waiting for the previous copy). The design that removes the round trip (route and plan on the device, host queues a pass ahead) is in the spec.

- **2026-11-04 (15): prompt path 2, stage 1 - the MoE of a long prompt routed and planned on the device: +9-11%, on by default (`SHADOW_PREFILL_DEVPLAN=0` off; only with `--tensor-prompt`).** New kernels (`kernels/forward.cu`): `route_topk_rows` (`route()` per token on the GPU: f64 exponentials, the sum in index order, top 10 by score then index, weights as f32 - checked against the host router on real logits with `SHADOW_DEVPLAN_CHECK=1`: 0 of 30,010 experts and 0 of 30,010 weights differ, histograms equal, every layer), `plan_scan` and `plan_fill` (the groups of 32 tokens per expert and the matrix addresses, in the layout `mma_grouped_*` read, with room for the most groups a phase can have; the empty ones are skipped by the kernel). The host no longer downloads the router logits, routes, builds or uploads a plan: it sends 512 expert addresses and flags per layer (they do not depend on the routing) and queues the layer. Two things had to change for the host to run ahead of the GPU: (1) the next layer's copy fills the half the layer before it reads, so the copy stream now waits for an event recorded after that layer's combine (without it: NaN logits, `argmax` panic), (2) the pinned read-ahead buffers are three, layer l in `l % 3`, each with its own done event - with two, the host blocked 60-87 ms a layer on the copy it had just queued before it could queue the next layer's compute (first device-planned build: -8%). **Prompt tok/s, device plan / host plan, interleaved pairs, default build, ctx 24,576: 9.5k 448.0, 483.2, 490.0, 491.3 / 443.0, 448.3, 446.5, 454.3, 456.5; 15k 510.2, 510.0, 517.3, 513.7 / 450.4, 460.0, 460.8, 461.6, 464.9 (+9% / +11.5%)**; one cold first run 407 / 416 (page cache). Checks: `--exact` reply hash 162a8e07 and `quality_test` 1.000 (12 of 12) unchanged, default-mode hash f35749c5 unchanged, fast-mode agreement 0.219 unchanged, `task_check` 42/48 unchanged, decode after a 9.5k prompt 33.7 (first reply) / 33.8 (follow-up) tok/s. The CUDA `exp()` is not the host library's, so the device routing is a fast-mode kernel in principle (it matched bit for bit on every logit checked).

- **2026-11-04 (16): prompt path 2, stage 2 analysis - what gates each layer's copy; `--direct-io` crashes (heap corruption).** Stage-1 timeline (`bench/nsys-stage1-2026-11-04`): 92 big copies in the window (1.9 passes, 5.0 s a pass), copy engine busy 3.3 s of each, **idle 1.2 s a pass**; the gap after the previous copy ended is 5.8 ms median but 36.8 ms mean: **68.3 ms on average (28 copies, 1.9 s) where an attention layer ran between two copies, 22.7 ms (63 copies, 1.4 s) otherwise**. The copy of layer l + 1 may not start before layer l - 1's experts are done (two stage halves), and an attention layer's chain (sparse attention ~100 ms, then its experts) makes that late; the copies run at 13-15 GB/s while active, 71 ms each, against ~41 ms of kernels a layer, so compute and copy are about equal and the dependency chain, not either's throughput, leaves the overlap short (perfect overlap would be ~3.3 s a pass instead of 5.0: +50%). Candidates: a ring of expert slots so a copy needs only its own slots free (stage 4), a faster copy (the late layers' 115-180 file-only experts are read by the CPU through the page cache: ~0.9 GB of DRAM traffic a layer beside a 15 GB/s DMA). **`--direct-io` (the uncommitted `DirectIoPool` in `readpool.rs`, not mine) crashed the first long-prompt run: `shadow.exe` APPCRASH 17:35:13, exception 0xC0000374 (heap corruption) in ntdll, exit -1073740940; the same build without the flag ran 365.7 tok/s on that launch (a slow one, 15k).** Not investigated; it may interact with the three read-ahead buffers (buffers are now `l % 3`, each with a done event). Also: my benchmark scripts killed every process named `shadow` between runs, which would stop a Shadow the user was running - they now kill only the test server by its port.

- **2026-11-04 (17): `--direct-io` crash: reproduced three times, cause not found.** `shadow.exe` APPCRASH 0xC0000374 (heap corruption, same fault offset 0x112fc5 in ntdll) at 17:35:13, 17:45:23 and 18:06:27, each the first long prompt after a start with `--direct-io` and the default 36 GiB tier (RAM tier filled, `Direct I/O enabled` printed); the same launches without the flag ran. Not default-build bugs: nothing in the default path was involved. `crates/shadow-model/examples/direct_io_stress.rs` (new) drives `DirectIoPool` alone with 540 unaligned pieces of ~850 KB per batch on the real model file and compares every byte with ordinary reads: 6 batches, 0 differences, no crash, so the pool itself holds up with ordinary heap buffers; the corruption needs something in the engine's use (pinned destination buffers, the read-ahead's submit / wait order, other readers sharing the pool, the tier). Runs I made with a 4 GiB tier the same afternoon are void: my port-based kill did not stop the previous server and four launches talked to one stale server. `--direct-io` is Gemini's uncommitted code (`readpool.rs`); do not run it on long prompts until someone finds this.

- **2026-11-04 (18): `--direct-io` crash, run with device planning off (`SHADOW_PREFILL_DEVPLAN=0`): crashed too (18:14:27, 0xC0000374)** - so it does not depend on stage 1. A crashed `shadow.exe` stays alive behind the Windows Error Reporting window (`WerFault`) holding its 42.8 GB until someone closes the window or kills it (9 minutes here): my test script now also kills any `shadow*` launched with its `--port`.

- **2026-11-04 (19): the `--direct-io` crash, narrowed (not fixed).** Whole runs (`--direct-io`, 3k prompt twice then 9.5k, default flags): tier 8 GiB **ok** (86-97 tok/s), 24 GiB **ok** (127-152), 32 GiB **crash**, 36 GiB **crash** (x4), also with `SHADOW_PREFILL_DEVPLAN=0`, with the read-ahead buffers pre-sized to 512 experts (`SHADOW_STAGE_SLOTS=512`: no buffer is ever replaced), and with every read-ahead waited right after it is submitted (`SHADOW_READ_SYNC=1`: nothing in flight). So it is not stage 1, not the buffer replacement and not an unfinished read. `SHADOW_HEAPCHECK=1|2` (new, debugging) validates the process heap at the long-prompt path's steps: valid at every check up to layer 0's `moe_batch`; the crash comes right after, while layer 1 is queued in the first pass; the exception differs between runs (0xC0000374 heap corruption, once 0xC0000005 access violation) - a write to memory the process does not own. `DirectIoPool` alone does not reproduce it (`examples/direct_io_stress.rs`: 540 fixed pieces; 120 batches of 1-400 pieces of 0.4-1.3 x 850 KB; 12 batches of 3,000 tiny 100-8,100 byte reads - all bytes equal, no crash). Crash dumps are written to C:/Users/a3k6g/AppData/Local/CrashDumps/shadow.exe.*.dmp (also one from Nov 3 19:26, before today's changes); no debugger is installed to read them (cdb / WinDbg), so the faulting function is not known.

- **2026-11-04 (20): the `--direct-io` crash found and fixed (WinDbg installed from Microsoft via winget; `cdb -z shadow.exe.35920.dmp` with `shadow.pdb`).** The dump's stack: `GpuSession::reset` -> `Model::ple_prefetch` -> `DirectIoPool::read_many` -> `submit` -> `wait_locked` -> `Vec::grow_one` -> `RtlReAllocateHeap` access violation. Cause (in `DirectIoPool`, Gemini's uncommitted code): `alloc_slot` takes a new 1 MiB `VirtualAlloc` slot for every 512 KiB piece when none is free - `ple_prefetch` reads thousands of tiny n-gram rows in one batch, so a batch committed thousands of MiB; with a 32+ GiB tier pinned the commit ran out, `submit` returned `Err` (the allocation failed) **while the reads it had already queued were still in flight into the caller's row buffers**; `ple_prefetch` then freed those buffers on the error return, and the next call's `wait_locked` copied the finished reads into freed memory: heap corruption in whatever allocated there (hence the varying exception and the later crash site; and why 8 and 24 GiB tiers, which leave commit, survived). Fix, `readpool.rs`: (1) `submit` waits for everything it queued before it returns an error; (2) at most `DIRECT_MAX_SLOTS` (128) slots, the oldest read is finished first (`retire_oldest`); (3) a failing direct batch is read by the buffered workers instead (`direct_failed` prints once), and `wait_async` waits on both paths. Checks: `direct_io_stress` (3,000 tiny reads x 6; 30 random batches) all bytes equal; `shadow serve --direct-io`, default 36 GiB tier, two 3k prompts and a 9.5k prompt: 198.1 / 260.7 / 241.6 tok/s, no crash, no `direct-I/O read failed`. (Direct I/O is still half the speed of the buffered path on this PC, as the earlier benchmark found: opt-in stays.) `readpool.rs` also holds Gemini's uncommitted work, so the file is not committed here; a copy of it before my edit is in the scratch folder.

- **2026-11-04 (21): why the layer copies run at 14 GB/s - the CPU's file reads - and the float kernels on by default.** `tests/scatter_probe.rs` (new, `--ignored`): 400-460 pieces of 2.4 MB from random offsets of a 4-16 GiB registered region, one `cuMemcpyHtoDAsync` each / one `cuMemcpyBatchAsync` (the engine's way) / adjacent / one contiguous block, and while device-to-device copies keep VRAM saturated: **27.6-28.1 GB/s in every case** - how the copies are cut and issued, and kernels using VRAM, cost nothing. In the engine's timeline the layer copies run at 14-18 GB/s (min 9.8); with the file reads skipped (`SHADOW_STAGE_NOREAD=1`, timing only; `bench/nsys-noread-2026-11-04`) **the median layer copy is 27.4 GB/s (p10 21.3, p90 27.9; 65% of the copies at 24+)** and the 9 s window has kernels busy 64.6%. So the one thing between this pass and the link's rate is the CPU copying the 7.5% of experts that are in no tier from the page cache into the read-ahead buffers (read + write of ~0.45 GB a late layer beside a DMA of 1.1 GB: 2.0 GB of DRAM traffic a layer, against DRAM that has ~35 GB/s). Prompt tok/s, 9.5k / 15k, two runs each: default 480.5 / 476.0 and 478.4 / 511.6; **float attention + float index scores 489.9 / 508.1 and 498.5 / 530.1 (+2-7%: with the device planner the kernels are on the critical path, they tied before)**; plus no file reads 526.3 / 577.1 and 494.5 / 576.7 (+8-17% more, not obtainable: the bytes must come from the file). So `SHADOW_ATT_F32` and `SHADOW_IDX_F32` are now on by default with `--tensor-prompt` (`=0` off). Checks: `--exact` hash 162a8e07 and 12 of 12, fast-mode agreement 0.219, `task_check` 42/48, three long prompts' agreement with `--exact` 0.165 / 0.063 / 0.304 (mean 0.177; double kernels 0.026 / 0.063 / 0.138 and float 0.046 / 0.063 / 0.018 in earlier runs: noise), default prompt speed 475.5 / 490.4 on that run. **Where the gap to Strata (934 / 1,422 tok/s at 9.6k / 15k) comes from:** Strata reads 15k in 2 passes (8k chunks); at our 14-28 GB/s that is 3-6 s of copying for 10.6 s total, i.e. its whole cost per token is ~0.7 ms, ours ~2 ms (kernels 0.75 ms + the copy-bound remainder). Passing it needs both fewer passes (8k rows: the per-row memory must come down from ~410 KB to ~100-150 KB) and ~2x faster kernels; neither is a tuning step.

- **2026-11-04 (22): prompt path 2, lean rows - a pass of 8,192 rows in sub-chunks of 2,048; on by default.** The layer's temporaries (the attention / DeltaNet sets, the router, the expert intermediates: ~410 KB a row) are only live inside one layer, so they are now sized for a sub-chunk (`SHADOW_SUB_ROWS`, 2,048) and a pass runs layer by layer over its sub-chunks (the first takes the remainder, so the last is a full 2,048); only the residual streams and each row's normalized input to the next layer are kept for the whole pass (`rbig`, `rnbig`: 2 x 4 x 2,560 floats = 82 KB a row; `bb.r` / `bb.rn` are views of the sub-chunk being run). The layer's experts are copied once for the whole pass (the copy pipeline advances after the layer's last sub-chunk; `moe_staged` returns early before it for the others), DeltaNet's state and the attention caches advance in position order, the drafter takes the last sub-chunk's features. A pass of 8,192 rows takes 1.55 GB of VRAM (temporaries 0.88 + 0.67) where 4,096 rows took 1.76. Two things found on the way: with the next layer's normalized input not carried between sub-chunks `hc_read_batch` / `hc_write_batch` fall back to a launch a token (110,592 `rmsnorm_groups` launches, prompts at 230 tok/s) - hence `rnbig`; and `batch_rows()` is now the pass size, not the temporaries' rows. Numerics: `--exact` hash 162a8e07 and 12 of 12 unchanged, default-mode hash identical for the unsplit pass, 4,096 and 8,192 rows (1bd164af), fast-mode agreement 0.219, `task_check` 42/48, decode after a 9.5k prompt 40.7 (first reply) / 33.5 tok/s. **Prompt tok/s, 9.5k / 15k:** unsplit 4,096 rows 503.2 / 501.3; two sub-chunks of 2,048 (4,096) 510.1 / 471.2 and 508.8 / 499.2; **8,192 rows 543.4 / 522.0, 545.4 / 525.5, then 537.5 / 509.0 in the checked default build**. Queueing the next layer's copy at the layer's start instead of after its last sub-chunk (`SHADOW_STAGE_EARLY`, on) ties with after (545.4 / 525.5 against 542.2 / 524.6). Timeline at 8,192 rows (`bench/nsys-lean8k-2026-11-04`, 6.6 s window): 45.4 GB copied (96.7 before), kernels busy 65.6%, copies 44.6%, neither 10.8%, copy with no kernel 23.6%, ~100 gaps of 10-50 ms (1.8 s) left, one a layer-pass. Sparse attention (`attention_group_sel_rows_f32` 21.8%, `attention_heads_rows` 17.7%, `index_scores_rows_f32` 4.4%) is now 44% of the kernel time.
- **2026-11-05 (23): the dense float attention kernel for a prompt's first positions (committed a50cfe3).** `attention_group_dense_rows_f32` (`kernels/forward.cu`): the float group kernel without token lists (row r attends positions 0 .. ntok0 + r - 1), 4 heads a block sharing each key row's loads; replaces `attention_heads_rows` (one head a block, 17.7% of the kernel time) when `--tensor-prompt` and `SHADOW_ATT_F32` are on. `--exact` hash 162a8e07 unchanged. Prompt tok/s 9.5k / 15k, float / double: 550.6 / 481.7 and 544.9 / 535.7 against 532.5 / 448.8 and 522.6 / 511.9 (+3-7%).
- **2026-11-05 (24): Nsight Compute on the sparse float attention kernel, and `attention_group_sel_rows_v2` built from it: +5-9% on prompts, on by default (`SHADOW_ATT_V2=0` keeps the first float kernel).** Profile of `attention_group_sel_rows_f32` (9.5k prompt, grid 6 x 32, 827 us a launch; `bench/ncu_attn_sel.ncu-repz`): FP32 FMA rate ~6% of peak, issue slots busy 31.5%, LSU requests 71% of peak and L1/TEX 72% - bound by load issue, not arithmetic nor DRAM (2.9%); every q value came from shared memory for 4 FMAs, the weighted sum read 1 shared score per FMA; and `block_sum` / `block_max`'s two 8 KB double buffers made the static shared memory 33.8 KB, so 2 blocks an SM (theoretical occupancy 33%) with 8 syncs per reduction per head. `v2`: 4 heads a block (fixed), scores 4 tokens a thread (a q load feeds 16 FMAs), max and sum by warp shuffles (shared memory 21 KB), the exp folded into the weighted sum's tile load (one pass over the scores less), and the weighted sum with a float4 of V columns a thread for 4 heads, the tile's rows split over 4 thread groups and summed at the end (16 FMAs per shared load). **Prompt tok/s, v2 / first float kernel, interleaved pairs, 9.5k: 540.8, 579.8, 578.7 / 540.3, 546.0, 520.5; 15k: 564.3, 572.7, 570.4 / 538.2, 539.3, 525.1** (medians 579 / 571 against 540 / 538: +7% / +6%); an earlier round 571.3 / 571.3 against 548.2 / 525.0 and one disturbed pair (472.7 / 427.3 against 534.2 / 418.2, a cold start). Numerics: float, so inexact like its predecessor: `quality_test` agreement 0.313 and 1 of 12 identical for both kernels (the default mode), the 160-token reply of the 9.5k summary prompt agrees with the first float kernel's for 253 characters and both are coherent; the `--exact` path does not use it.

- **2026-11-05 (28): a 15k prompt in one pass (residual streams in parts, `rnbig` gone) and 16 read workers.** (1) The normalized input to the next layer is no longer kept for the whole pass: each sub-chunk normalizes its own at its start (`rmsnorm_groups_rows`, one launch for all rows, the arithmetic of the per-row launches; it also replaces the per-row loop in `hc_read_batch` when the norm is not carried), so a pass costs 41 KB a row of residual streams, not 82. (2) The residual streams are one buffer of `rows` rows a *part*, carved wherever the lent ranges have room (the first sub-chunk, the remainder, ends part 0): the two lent ranges were 0.878 + 0.421 GiB, and one 0.6 GB buffer did not fit in either. Pass rows for the 15k prompt: 11,008 -> 11,968 (no `rnbig`) -> **16,384 (parts): one pass of 15,136 rows**, 9.5k one pass of 9,536. Speed barely moved at 15k (667 -> 670: the first pass was already compute-bound, the second small), so passes are no longer what limits it. (3) `READ_WORKERS` 8 -> 16 (`SHADOW_READ_WORKERS`): the SSD wants a deeper queue. Prompt tok/s at 8 / 16 / 24 / 32 / 48 workers: 9.5k first prompt 660.8 / 677.3 / 680.3 / 679.5 / 679.3, repeat 686.2 / 690.4 / 689.2 / 689.6 / 689.9, 15k 666.5 / 682.5 / 685.3 / 683.5 / 684.8 (+2.3%; 4 workers 636.9 / 683.9 / 648.9). `SHADOW_MAIN_PRIO=2` (the queueing thread at highest priority) 663.0 / 685.4 / 633.0: no help. Checks: `--exact` hash 162a8e07 and `quality_test` 12 of 12, fast-mode agreement 0.381, `task_check` 42/48; decode after a 9.5k prompt with 1,024-row sub-chunks 40.4 / 38.9 and 41.0 / 39.4 tok/s against 39.0 / 37.4 and 39.1 / 37.5 with 2,048 (36.6 / 37.4 in the last run after the worker change, to be re-measured). **The 15k timeline (`bench/nsys-steady15-2026-11-05`, `tools/nsys_last_run.py`, `tools/nsys_gap_why.py`): 15.4 s window, kernels busy 67.1%, copies 11.3%, 419 gaps of 10-50 ms = 4.3 s + 76 of 1-10 ms = 0.7 s; the host's `cuLaunchKernel` is 5 us (median) but 1,011 launches a run take ~16 ms (one Windows scheduler quantum): those are the gaps.**
- **2026-11-05 (29): the ~16 ms launch stalls - what they are not.** In the steady 15k timeline 675 of 36,348 `cuLaunchKernel` calls take >5 ms (typical launch 5 us), exactly one every 15-16 ms (317 at 16 ms, 277 at 15 ms), with ~20 fast launches between them; the GPU is idle meanwhile (no kernel, memset or copy), one thread makes every CUDA call and no other thread's call overlaps, the call before is always `cuCtxSetCurrent`. They are the gaps: ~10 ms of idle after each sub-chunk step (419 gaps of 10-50 ms = 4.3 s of 15.4 s at 15k; the count scales with the number of sub-chunk steps). A plain CuPy launch loop (60,000 launches, GPU otherwise idle) has none (max 0.1 ms). Tested, all on `prio.sh` pairs with 9.5k first / repeat and 15k: `SHADOW_MAIN_PRIO=2` (queueing thread at highest priority) 663.0 / 685.4 / 633.0 against 660.8 / 686.2 / 666.5; `SHADOW_FLUSH_EVERY=N` (a `cuStreamQuery` every N launches, skipped during graph capture, which it would invalidate) N=2 / 8 / 32: 676.5 / 690.3 / 680.9, 677.7 / 687.3 / 655.6, 679.5 / 653.3 / 683.5 against 677.4 / 689.9 / 683.4 for none; `SHADOW_TIMER_MS=1` (`timeBeginPeriod(1)`) 678.2 / 690.1 / 683.1 and 680.5 / 692.2 / 680.0 against 673.8 / 688.8 / 680.5. All ties: they stay opt-in (inert by default). Facts about the machine: WDDM, hardware-accelerated GPU scheduling already on (`HwSchMode` 2), GeForce RTX 5070 with a display attached (no TCC), 15 other processes hold the GPU for graphics (Discord, Steam, Edge, Claude, explorer). Open: a GPU-scheduler trace (GPUView / WPA) of one stall, and graphs that cover a whole sub-chunk layer (docs/PLAN_EXCEED_STRATA.md).

In the whole-prompt timeline (graphs traced) ten kernels with double sums took 3.0 s of 12.3 s of kernel time: `delta_steps128` 972 ms, `hc_write_rmsnorm_rows` 484, `conv_silu_steps_par` 276, `rmsnorm_groups_rows` 242, `l2norm_qk_rows` 227, `head_norm_rope_qgk_rows` 204 (rope in double: left), `hc_mix_rows` 199 (float already), `gated_rmsnorm_rows_w` 151. New `_f` kernels without any double, sums by warp shuffles (`block_sum_f`): `rmsnorm_groups_rows_f`, `hc_write_rmsnorm_rows_f`, `l2norm_qk_rows_f`, `gated_rmsnorm_rows_wf`, `conv_silu_steps_par_f`, `delta_steps128_f` (the two sums over the 128 rows in float, 8 partials of 16). The float norms differ from the double ones by ~1e-7 relative, 4 orders of magnitude below the fp16 tensor-core products fast mode already carries, so I read the shift as the chaos of near-tied tokens rather than drift, but the metric cannot tell the two apart: `SHADOW_NORM_F32=0` is the way back.

- **2026-11-06 (45): a 15k prompt took two passes - sub-chunks fitted to the prompt put it in one (+13%, default; `SHADOW_ONE_PASS=0` off).** The 15k prompt's profile (entry 44) showed 124.7 GB copied host-to-device, 2.4x the experts' 47 GiB. `SHADOW_STAGE_TIME=1` printed why: `BIGBATCH ... rows 2048 of cap 16384, pass 12288 rows`. The lent room (1.36 GiB for the layer temporaries of one 2,048-row sub-chunk plus the residual streams of the whole pass) fits only 12,288 rows, so 15,136 rows ran as 12,288 + 2,848 and every layer's experts were copied (and the file-only ones read from the SSD) twice. Entry 28's "15k in one pass" stopped being true when the room shrank (MMQ buffers, PLE and other additions). Sub-chunk size sweep, 15k, two servers x two prompts each, tok/s: 2,048 (pass 12,288): 977, 878, 976, 878 (mean 927); 1,792 (pass 14,336, still two passes): 920, 812, 920, 866 (880); 1,536 (pass 16,384, one pass): 1,076, 1,082, 1,106, 947 (1,053, +13.6%). A 9.5k prompt (one pass either way) at 1,536 vs 2,048: 1,007, 1,032, 1,005, 1,135 (1,045) vs 1,023, 1,009, 1,047, 1,179 (1,064): -1.8%, inside the spread. So `big_batch_on` now plans the sub-chunk size with the prompt length (`hint_prompt`): if the pass at 2,048 rows is shorter than the prompt, it tries 1,792, 1,536, 1,280, 1,024, 768 and takes the first that holds the whole prompt (or the longest pass); prompts that already fit are untouched; an explicit `SHADOW_SUB_ROWS` is obeyed. The first prompt after a server restart is still 5-10% slower than the second in the other runs, and this machine's absolute numbers moved ~10% between sessions (the 15k default read 878-977 today against 1,158 on 2026-11-05), so only pairs from the same hour mean anything. - **2026-11-06 (46): the gate/up experts on llama.cpp's int8 kernels (IQ2_S, IQ3_XXS, IQ3_S): a tie, opt-in (`SHADOW_MMQ_MOE=1`).** `shadow_mmq_moe` (`native/mmq/moe_part.inc`, included by `shadow_mmq.cu`; `mmq.rs` `moe`, `session.rs` `moe_gate_up`): a copy of ggml's `mul_mat_q` launch that reads each group's matrix from the planner's own address table (experts live in cache slots and the stage halves, so no contiguous expert tensor) and takes its tokens from the planner's group records (`plan_fill`: count, input rows, output rows); the activations are quantized once for the gate and up groups with a gather (`moe_ids` + ggml's `quantize_mmq_q8_1` with ids) into the layer's dead aliased attention / DeltaNet region (no extra VRAM); `mul_mat_q_process_tile` is ggml's. Runs and answers correctly (9.5k reply coherent, no fallback panics). Nsight Systems, 15k prompt in one pass, graphs off, two prompts per run, window = the last prompt: **old kernels** `mma_grouped_iq2_s/iq3_xxs/iq3_s` 1,065 + 1,008 + 594 = 2,667 ms (878 launches, 3.04 ms each), kernel busy 9.22 s of 9.97 s; **int8, 32-token groups** `shadow_moe_q` 2,400 ms (2.73 ms) + `quantize_mmq_q8_1` 261 ms (dense + gather; the dense-only run has ~100 ms), busy 9.11 s of 9.61 s; **int8, 64-token groups (`SHADOW_MMA_GROUP=64`)** `shadow_moe_q` 2,169 ms (2.47 ms) but the down projection's `mma_grouped64_iq4_nl` 1,100 ms (990 at 32), quantize 364 ms, busy 9.11 s of 9.88 s. Net kernel time -1.2% in both: a tie (the standalone benchmark's 1.7-2.4x does not carry over). Wall-clock A/Bs in tok/s were inside the noise (this machine's unprofiled 15k runs read 738-1,114 on a fresh server, the second prompt 908-993 whichever arm; profiled runs 1,064-1,268): 9.5k on 1,030, 1,074, 1,031, 1,089 vs off 1,019, 938, 1,019, 968 (+7%, but pairs of one hour each), 15k first prompt on 942, 948 vs off 1,076, 997; so not a measured win. **Why it is not 2x:** the standalone benchmark gave every expert one tile of exactly its 40 tokens; here a 32-token group of an expert with ~40 tokens per sub-chunk leaves ~37% of the columns empty (64-token groups: ~38%), the padded layout quantizes 36.9k columns for 20.5k real ones (the +160-250 ms), and an empty-group block still launches (grid 2G x row tiles). Possible follow-up (not done): ggml's own expert layout, compact columns by prefix sum of the group counts and J-wide tiles of the expert's real count (`expert_bounds`), which would remove the padding and most of the quantize time; worth at most ~4-5% of a 15k prompt. Without the flag nothing changes. Also learned: a 15k prompt in the same server after a 9.5k one reuses the cached 9.5k prefix (5,609 tokens read the second time) - an A/B that runs p9k then p15k twice has only one valid 15k sample per server.

- **2026-11-06 (47): gate + up + SwiGLU in one kernel for the grouped experts: bit-identical, slower, opt-in (`SHADOW_GU_SWIGLU=1`).** `mma_grouped_gu_*` / `mma_grouped64_gu_*` (`kernels/matvec.cu`, `mma_core2`): a block dequantizes a 64-row tile of the gate matrix and the same tile of the up matrix for the same tokens, runs both products on the same activation tile and writes `silu(gate) * up` straight into `bb.act` (same fp32 accumulation and the same `x * (1 / (1 + expf(-x)))` as `swiglu`), so the 2 x k x ff floats of gate / up outputs a token, their write and the `swiglu` pass over them (routed experts) are gone; `session.rs` `gate_up_swiglu`. The 9.5k reply is byte-identical to the unfused path (hash 8a4a7074 both). Nsight Systems, 15k prompt, one pass, graphs off, last prompt's window: **unfused** `mma_grouped_iq2_s/iq3_xxs/iq3_s` 1,065 + 1,008 + 594 = 2,667 ms plus `swiglu` 175 ms (routed + shared), kernel busy 9.22 s of 9.97 s; **fused** `mma_grouped_gu_iq2_s/iq3_xxs/iq3_s` 1,238 + 1,093 + 682 = 3,013 ms (3.46 / 3.42 / 3.41 ms a launch against 2.97 / 3.15 / 2.97) and `swiglu` below 1.7% (the shared experts'), kernel busy 9.37 s of 10.12 s: **+1.6% kernel time, a loss**. The fused kernel's two weight tiles and two accumulator sets (27.6 KB of shared memory a block, 2x the registers of `mma_core`) cost more than the 79 MB of gate / up writes and reads it saves (the routed `swiglu` was a bandwidth-bound ~120 us a launch). Wall-clock tok/s in unprofiled A/Bs were inside the noise again (15k: 1,126 fused / 1,131 unfused; 9.5k 943 / 895); profiled: 1,117, 1,219 fused against 1,129, 1,064 unfused (graphs off). Remaining small-kernel items measured in entry 44 (hc_mix_rows 2.0%, hc_write_rmsnorm 3.2%, hc_down_inject 2.9%) are within ~1.5x of their traffic roofline on this GPU; the hc up + mix fusion would save at most the 193 ms of `hc_mix_rows` (2%) at the price of a custom 4-stream epilogue in the tile kernel - not done.

To test that without writing ggml's compact layout, `mma_grouped48_*` (`mma_core<Q, 48>`, records of 97 u32), `mma_group()` accepting 48 and `shadow_mmq_moe` taking `chunk` 48 give groups that fit the average expert in one tile at ~83% fill. The int8 gate/up kernel gains only ~10% per launch from 62% to 83% fill and the fp16 down kernel loses more than that at 48 or 64, so the kernel is not limited by the padding; its 2.5 ms a launch (the standalone benchmark's 1.55-1.7 ms for 40 tokens an expert on 512 experts) is the cost of unpacking the IQ2_S / IQ3 grids into the int8 tile and the per-group launch grid. **Closed for the prompt path's MoE products:** the tensor-core fp16 kernels, the int8 kernels (entry 46), 64- and 48-token groups and the fused gate/up/SwiGLU (entry 47) all sit within 1-2% of each other. Unprofiled wall-clock at 48 groups with int8 on: profiled 1,140 and 1,240 tok/s, as the others.

- **2026-11-06 (49): the DeltaNet recurrence on 512 threads, four to a column of the state (-45% kernel time, -3.4% of the 15k prompt's kernel time; bit-identical; default for the float path, `SHADOW_DELTA_F4=0` keeps the 128-thread kernel).** Nsight Compute (`bench/ncu_k_delta.ncu-repz`, the double kernel) had the recurrence at 48 blocks of 128 threads (one block a value head on 48 SMs): 255 registers a thread with 17 MB of local-memory spills, 4 warps an SM (8% achieved occupancy), 0.6 instructions a cycle, 6.7 cycles between issues of a warp: latency-bound, not throughput-bound. The state column `S[:, j]` evolves independently of the others, and the only cross-row work is two sums over the 128 rows, so `delta_steps128_f4` splits each column over 4 threads (32 rows in 32 registers, no spills; 16 warps an SM). To keep the output bit-identical the sums keep `delta_steps128_f`'s association: each thread makes the partial sums of its two 16-row slices, every thread of the column gathers all eight with `__shfl_sync` and adds them in the original order. Per-token beta / decay are computed once per block; k and q sit in shared memory with 4 floats of padding per 32 and are read as float4 (8 loads instead of 32); the next token's q, k, v, b, a are fetched into registers before the current token computes. Steps on the way (Nsight Systems, 15k prompt in one pass, graphs off, `delta_steps128*` time in the last prompt's window): `delta_steps128_f` **763 ms** (366 launches, 2.08 ms) -> 4 threads a column alone 867 ms (2.37 ms; slower) -> a probe that skipped the global loads 1.89 ms a launch (the loads were a fifth of it) -> **with float4 shared reads and the next-token prefetch 422 ms (1.15 ms)**. Kernel busy 8.91 s of 9.65 s against 9.22 s of 9.97 s before (-3.4%). The 9.5k reply is byte-identical (hash 8a4a7074 before and after); `--exact` hash 162a8e07 unchanged (the double kernels are untouched). Wall-clock tok/s, new / old kernel, A-B-B-A servers: 9.5k 1,070, 985, 1,047, 1,056 (mean 1,040) / 1,027, 955, 1,029, 1,065 (1,019), +2%; 15k (first prompt of a server) 1,083, 1,069 / 1,078, 1,066: +0.4% - the profile's 3.4% kernel-time gain mostly does not show in the wall clock, because the 15k prompt is partly paced by the SSD reads and the noise of this machine is ±8%.

Scores (fp32 sums of fp16 products) go to the same scratch, the per-head maxima by shuffles + `wred2`; softmax and the weighted sum are `_v3`'s. Nsight Systems, 15k prompt in one pass, graphs off: `attention_group_sel_rows_v3` 1,018 ms (2,471 launches, 412 us) -> `_tc` **678 ms (274 us)**; kernel busy 8.57 s of 9.32 s against 8.91 s of 9.65 s (-3.8%). Fidelity (fp16 q and k; the reply to the 9.5k summary changes its wording, hash 8a4a7074 -> 50a172d7, still coherent): `tools/needle_test.py` (new; a code sentence buried at 8 positions of a 9.6k-token prompt, no prompt cache): default fast mode 8/8, tensor-core scores 8/8, `--exact` 8/8; with `multi` (four "access code of project X" sentences, one asked for, 12 prompts): default fast 12/12, tensor-core scores 12/12. Both are saturated, so they show no loss, not equal fidelity; `--exact` is untouched (hash 162a8e07 by construction: the double kernels). Not done: the weighted-sum phase (the other ~270 us a launch) on tensor cores; the dense float attention (`attention_group_dense_rows_f32`, 3.2%) the same way.

- **2026-11-06 (51): the sparse attention's weighted sum on the tensor cores: slower than the FMA version, opt-in (`SHADOW_ATT_TC=2`).** `attention_group_sel_rows_tc2` (`att_v3_body<true, true>`): out[head][dim] = sum over positions of p[head][pos] V[pos][dim] as an m16n8k16 product, the weights in fp16 shared memory ([head][position of the 256-position tile], rows past the group's heads zero), a warp a 32-dim slice, V loaded straight from the cache as scalars (a lane: one dim of two positions for each of four 8-dim tiles) and packed to f16x2; sums in fp32. Nsight Systems, 15k prompt, one pass, graphs off, the kernel in the last prompt's window: `_tc` (FMA weighted sum) **678 ms (274 us a launch)** -> `_tc2` **750 ms (303 us)**, +11%; with the position loop unrolled by 4 (to batch the scalar V loads) 817 ms (331 us), worse still; kernel busy 8.62 s of 9.37 s against 8.57 s of 9.32 s. The reply stays coherent (hash a517bdff, the opening words of the scores-only variant's reply). The FMA path's V float4 loads feed 48 FMAs each; the tensor-core path needs 16 scalar V loads and 8 conversions a lane for every 4 mma, so it moves fewer instructions but waits on more loads, and nothing hides that latency (one 256-position tile at a time, 8 warps). A tile-staged version (V rows into shared memory with `cp.async`, fp16, `ldmatrix.trans`) would be the way to try again; not built. Process note: `nsys export` does not overwrite an existing `.sqlite`, so re-profiling under the same name reads the old file (the first re-run of this profile did); delete the file first.

Runs and answers coherently (9.5k reply hash 0bf44790, identical across two servers). Nsight Systems, 15k prompt in one pass, graphs off, the kernel in the last prompt's window: `attention_group_dense_rows_f32` 278 ms (1,419 launches, 196 us) -> `_tc` **275 ms (193.6 us)**: a tie (-1%, inside the run-to-run spread of the profiles' kernel times), kernel busy 8.56 s of 9.31 s against 8.57 s of 9.32 s. The dense kernel's time is not in its scores: its softmax passes (`block_max` / `block_sum` over double conversions per head and the exp pass over the scratch) and the weighted sum (one value column a thread, 8 heads, a tile of 256 positions at a time) take the rest, and entry 51 showed that the weighted sum does not move to the tensor cores cheaply. So the default stays the float kernel. Process notes from this stretch: (1) a first run of this variant crashed once (the server died after its first reply, no message) while Strata was loading its 47 GiB at the same time (free RAM 0.8-8 GiB): not reproduced on three later runs with free RAM; (2) while another large process holds the RAM the engine falls back to a smaller tier or fails to load, so a test during that time (here three 503s of the profiled server) measures nothing.

- **2026-11-06 (53): a chat resumed from the stored shared-prefix snapshot answered with garbage - a CUDA graph replayed over buffers that had moved (fixed; found from a user's screenshot).** The symptom: in an agent chat (grep / glob / cmd tool turns) the web page's last turn read 6,915 tokens and answered `ORNNESS... // ... The user sent another message:` (33 tokens, no tool call); the saved chat (the page's localStorage, parsed from its LevelDB log) ends with an empty assistant message there. **Reproduction** (`tools/replay_snapshot.py`, `tools/prefix_restore_test.py`; the exact request was rebuilt by running the page's own `buildContextMessages` in Node on the saved history: 75 messages, 17 old tool outputs shortened by the page, ~11.1k tokens with the tool list): two short requests that share the system prompt and tools (the server keeps a snapshot of that start), then the chat's request: the server restores ~4.2k tokens and reads 6,912 (the user's session read 6,915). Reply on today's defaults: `FD369999999999FD3699...` repeated, deterministic. **What it is not:** the same request read cold (no prefix snapshot, 11,125 tokens), after a conversation snapshot (15.9k restored + 6k read), with `--exact`, with `SHADOW_NO_LEND=1`, with `SHADOW_BIG_ROWS=2048` or `4096`, `SHADOW_SUB_ROWS=1024` or `1536`, `SHADOW_ALIAS_ROWS=0` or `SHADOW_BATCH_GRAPHS=0` is correct; it is also garbled on the old defaults (`SHADOW_ATT_TC=0 SHADOW_DELTA_F4=0 SHADOW_ONE_PASS=0`), with `SHADOW_MMQ=0`, `SHADOW_PLE_BATCH=0`, `SHADOW_PREFILL_DEVPLAN=0` and `SHADOW_ATT_V3=0`, so none of the 2026-11-06 kernel changes. A GPU synchronize before the snapshot and before the restore changed nothing (tried, removed). **Bisect** (worktrees, the same reproduction): 0206863 and 8539f0c (11-05 13:55) correct; c6c8c23 (11-05 14:27) and every later build garbled. c6c8c23 changed one number: the default layer sub-chunk 1,024 -> 2,048 rows (STATUS 35). **Cause:** the prompt path replays a CUDA graph per (layer, row count, sub-chunk) and the key held one address, `bb.r.ptr` (the residual-stream view), as a stand-in for "where the batch buffers are". The other per-row buffers (`u`, `rn`, `qkv`, `x`, `eg`, `gtab`, ...) are carved from whichever stretch of the arena and the KV tail the prompt lends, and that stretch depends on the prompt (the KV tail kept = tokens + prompt length + 256, the arena part `want`): a graph captured for one prompt (4,221 tokens) was replayed for another (restored 4.2k + 6.9k) over buffers at other addresses, wherever the residual-stream part happened to land on the same address. With 2,048-row sub-chunks the row counts and views coincided between those two requests; with 1,024 or 1,536 they did not, which is why those hid it. Turning aliasing off moves the layout and also hid it (not understood further than that). **Fix:** `GpuSession::bb_sig`, a hash of the addresses of every big-batch buffer, every residual-stream part, `mmq_y` and `dscr`, computed when the big batch is installed (0 with the small batch) and mixed into the key in `batch_graph_segment`. A repeated prompt shape keeps the same layout, so its graphs are still reused. **Checks:** the reproduction on default settings: a relevant reply and a valid tool call at 1,063 tok/s (it was 1,170 garbled); `tools/prefix_restore_test.py` (a code word in one of three ~2k-token tool results after the restore, three items): known-bad build 178c15d **0 of 3**, fixed build **3 of 3**. This is a correctness bug that was in the default build from 11-05 14:27 until now: any long chat that started from the stored system-prompt snapshot (every new conversation after the first) with a prompt whose lent layout differed from the snapshot's request could answer with garbage. Not tested: the same fault with the decode graphs (`graph_segment`, keyed by layer only; their buffers are fixed).

The runs: today's 550, 531, 523, 534 (3k), 1,071, 1,055, 1,106, 1,041 (9.6k), 1,055, 927, 1,123, 1,046 (15k), decode 72.3, 75.8, 74.0, 78.9; baseline 479, 535, 515, 504, 861, 1,026, 944, 1,013, 833, 941, 826, 781, decode 78.3, 78.6, 77.9, 78.1. - **2026-11-06 (55): the desktop launcher (step F of the verification plan), checked from the code and the system; the tray Quit itself was not exercised.** The screen was not used (access to the launcher and the shell was declined). `shadow-ui.exe` (`crates/shadow-ui/src/main.rs`): closing its window **hides it to the tray** (`close_to_tray`, default on), it does not quit, and the server it started keeps running with its 36 GiB tier pinned; only the tray menu's Quit does `app.exit(0)`, whose `ExitRequested` handler (`stop_on_quit`, default on, only for a server the launcher itself started) sends `POST /shutdown` (the route exists in `shadow-serve`) and kills the child before exiting. A launcher killed by `Stop-Process` skips all of that (TerminateProcess): the server I had started through it survived exactly that way on 2026-11-06, which is therefore not a bug in Quit. Start Menu entry `Shadow.lnk` (target `target\release\shadow-ui.exe`, icon `crates\shadow-ui\icons\icon.ico`): the icon file is valid (6 images, 16 to 256 px), the shortcut's icon loads and the exe carries its own; how it looks in the menu was not seen. Open: Quit from the tray (does the server go and the RAM come back), and whether closing the window to the tray is what the user wants for a program that holds 36+ GiB of pinned RAM.

- **2026-11-07 (58): Phase 2 of the plan to beat Strata: where the time outside the layer loop goes, CUDA graphs on/off, and the 512-token path.** New `SHADOW_PHASES=1` (stderr, off by default, adds no synchronization when off): per pass the host time to embed the rows, to queue the layers, the device drained, and `answer`'s lend / passes / restore / snapshot. **15,170-token prompt, graphs off, one server:** prompt_ms 12.76 s (1,189 tok/s) = passes 11.75 s (embed rows 0.27, layers queued by 11.18, 0.22 more until the device is drained, observers 0.07) + `lend_cache` 0.44 + `restore_cache` 0.32 (1,813 evicted experts) + conversation snapshot 0.26 (0.9 GiB to host) + render/encode/fit_context 0.02: **~1.6 s outside the layer loop, not the 2.3 s of the plan** (each part 2-3%; none fixed yet). The layer loop, 11.2 s, is the lever (Phase 3). **CUDA graphs, new prompts, A-B-B-A twice = 8 fresh servers, no prompt cache, random ids, idle machine:** 15k: graphs on 1,239 / 1,131 / 1,254 / 1,073 (mean 1,174), off 1,295 / 1,275 / 1,299 / 1,298 (mean 1,292): **off +10%** and far steadier; 9.6k on 1,222 / 1,165 / 1,234 / 1,163 (1,196) against off 1,226 / 1,237 / 1,178 / 1,215 (1,214): +1.5%, a tie; 0.5k-2k tokens (one A-B-B-A, 3 prompts per size): ties (2.1k tokens 425.7 on, 425.0 off). Rule (a tie is a loss, what loses stays opt-in): **`SHADOW_BATCH_GRAPHS=1` now turns the prompt-path graphs on; default off** (a repeated shape still runs 10% faster with them, STATUS 30; nothing in a chat repeats one). Decode graphs (`SHADOW_GRAPHS`) are untouched. **Short prompts, a cliff:** below `LONG_PROMPT` = 1,024 unread tokens a prompt read in passes of 256 rows, each ~1.9 s whatever its size (every pass fetches most experts of every layer: 7.4 ms a token; 999 tokens 7.7 s, 623 tokens 5.0 s); from 1,024 one big pass cost ~4 s up to 2k tokens (its lend + restore ~0.65 s, a pass of 307 tokens already 3.2 s: the SSD floor of the 11 GiB not in the tier). New `SHADOW_LONG_PROMPT=n` override; A-B-B-A at 260-830 tokens (old path ms by tokens: 264 2,536; 394 3,418; 426 3,423; 523 4,007; 609 4,574; 661 5,137; 741 5,474; 785 5,780; new path: 307 3,916; 431 4,091; 458 4,053; 571 3,962; 604 4,021; 816 4,057, flat ~4.0 s, the first prompt on a server ~5 s): they meet at ~500 tokens, below it the old path is cheaper (264 tokens 2.5 s against 3.9 s). **`LONG_PROMPT` is now 512:** a 1,000-token prompt 7.7 s -> ~4.1 s, 700 tokens 5.3 -> 4.0. **Gates (machine idle, target-dev build):** `--exact` hash **162a8e07** and `quality_test` 12 of 12 with `SHADOW_LONG_PROMPT=1024` and with 512; four fixed prompts of 237-1,125 tokens (704 tokens takes the new path) give the same reply hashes under both (4bba9c11, 0bf034d6, 8a440074, fb60f1c3); `cargo test --release --workspace` 35 test binaries, no failures; needle 15k **8/8**; `prefix_restore_test` **3/3**; needle 20k `multi` **7/8**: the one miss (item at 0.66, MAPLE-6961 answered MAPLE-4800) is the same item with the new defaults, with graphs on, and with `SHADOW_ATT_TC=0 SHADOW_DELTA_F4=0` (the old kernels), so it belongs to that prompt on today's STATUS.md text (the needle prompts are built from it, and it has grown since STATUS 54's 8/8), not to these changes. **Not done:** the three 0.3-0.4 s fixed costs (lend, restore, snapshot) and the 0.27 s embedding loop (a synchronous upload per token); the 3.2-4.0 s floor of every prompt of 100-3,000 tokens is the SSD read of the experts the 36 GiB tier does not hold (Phase 5, the user's decision). Next: Phase 3, layer-wide MoE groups.

- **2026-11-07 (59): Phase 3 experiment (layer-wide MoE groups): the gate (MoE kernel time -25%) is not met at 80 tokens an expert, so nothing was built.** **(a) llama.cpp's int8 MMQ, standalone (`bench/mmq/mmq_*.exe`, this model's shapes: 512 experts, hidden 2,560, expert_ff 640), product time per token, microseconds:** gate/up IQ3_XXS 0.123 at 20 tokens an expert, 0.0755 at 40, **0.0513 at 80 (-32% against 40)**, 0.0473 at 160 (-37%), 0.0430 at 320 (-43%); down IQ4_NL 0.0846 at 40, 0.0624 at 160 (-26%); the quantize pass is a constant ~0.021 us a token. **(b) In the engine (Nsight Systems, `--cuda-graph-trace=node`, graphs off, 9.6k-token prompt, last prompt's window, the engine's own fp16 tensor-core `mma_grouped_*` kernels):** 2,048-row sub-chunks (40 tokens an expert, groups of 32): gate/up `iq2_s` 1,329 + `iq3_xxs` 1,224 + `iq3_s` 689 = **3,242 ms** (3.5-3.7 ms a launch), down `iq4_nl` **1,180 ms** (1.56 ms), `q2_0` 272; `SHADOW_SUB_ROWS=3072` (60 an expert, 32): 1,295 + 1,165 + 632 = 3,092 (-4.6%), down 1,097 (-7%); `SHADOW_SUB_ROWS=4096 SHADOW_MMA_GROUP=64` (80 an expert): 1,102 + 894 + 553 = **2,549 (-21%)** at 4.7 ms a launch (1.3x the time for 2x the tokens), down 1,079 (-9%). Both larger runs needed two passes (the lent room does not hold the pass at those sub-chunk sizes: 570-670 tok/s, 306-406 GB copied), so only their kernel times mean anything. **Reading:** 80 tokens an expert with 64-token groups takes ~0.8 s of the ~4.7 s MoE kernel time of a 9.6k prompt (-17%), ~1.2 s of a 15k prompt (9% of its 12.7 s); the standalone curve flattens after 80 (a further -9% at 160), so a 160-token, 128-group version would add little. That is below the plan's gate (-25%) and would cost a restructure of the layer loop (mixers per sub-chunk keeping `u`, one MoE over 4,096+ rows from buffers aliased onto the dead mixer temporaries: ~217 KB a row of MoE temporaries, +0.44 GB at 4,096 rows out of a lent room that already limits the pass), with the --exact and graph-free paths to re-verify. **Not built.** **What the experiment did show:** in the engine the same product costs more than in the standalone harness at the same 40 tokens an expert: IQ3_XXS 3.7 ms a launch (fp16 `mma_grouped`) against 1.55 ms (llama.cpp int8, standalone), and the engine's own int8 `shadow_moe_q` (STATUS 46) 2.7 ms: a factor 1.7-2.4 that has no explanation yet (ragged per-expert counts, expert weights scattered over cache slots and the stage halves, the resident / copied split into two launches). Finding it is a cheaper lever than the restructure (a standalone run with Zipf-distributed counts and scattered slots would show whether it is the data or the launch structure). Next: that, or Phase 1.

- **2026-11-07 (60): why the engine's MoE products run at twice the standalone speed: group count, not the engine; the fix on paper is group widths that match each expert's tokens (-30% predicted), not built.** **What a launch is:** one `mma_grouped_*` (or `shadow_moe_q`) launch per layer and sub-chunk does gate + up for every expert at once (grid.y = 2 x groups); the resident-expert launch of each pair is nearly empty (the cache is lent away: 20-400 us against 7.5 ms), so one launch covers the whole layer. **Real routing** (`SHADOW_MOE_STATS=1`, new, one line a layer): a 2,048-row sub-chunk makes 20,480 (token, expert) pairs over 360-470 active experts, counts min/p25/p50/p75/max 0/1-12/8-33/32-60/250-960, so **~900 groups of 32 tokens at 71% fill**. **Probes:** forced uniform routing (`SHADOW_ROUTE_UNIFORM=1`, new, wrong output) gives the same or slower launches (8.0-8.6 ms against 7.4-7.5 ms), so skew is not the cause. Nsight Compute on the saved real launch (`bench/ncu_k_moe_iq2s.ncu-repz`, 5.87 ms): DRAM 14.5%, L1/TEX 59%, L2 61%, tensor pipe 40%, 0.51 eligible warps a scheduler, 60% of the stall cycles on L1TEX scoreboard, and the stall samples sit on the `SHF`/`LOP3` instructions after the weight-decode loads (49%), `HMMA` 9%. **Standalone harnesses** (`bench/mma_bench.cu`: the engine's own `mma_grouped_*` kernels on tables in the planner's layout; `bench/mmq_moe_bench.cu`: `shadow_mmq_moe` from the DLL; builds in `bench/mmabench/`): they reproduce the engine's launch times (fp16 IQ3_XXS gate+up, 2,048 rows: uniform 8.5 ms, ragged 7.9 ms; int8 `shadow_moe_q`, ragged, chunk 32: 5.9 ms; engine 7.4 and 5.3-7.7 ms). The int8 kernel costs a near constant per group, **3.22 / 3.67 / 4.14 us at groups of 32 / 48 / 64** (a line: 2.30 us + 0.029 us a column), and the ragged counts make 922 / 740 / 655 groups: 5.94 / 5.43 / 5.42 ms. llama.cpp's own standalone run (3.1 ms for the same 40-token average) has one group an expert, 512 groups, uniform counts. So the 2x was never the engine: it is groups x per-group cost, and the 48/64-token groups of STATUS 48 gave only -8% because fewer groups are paid for by dearer ones and lower fill. **Model** (those three points reproduce to 2%): groups matched to each expert's count in widths 16 / 32 / 64 / 128 (full 128-wide groups, the remainder in the smallest width that holds it) make 545 groups at 74% fill: **predicted 4.1 ms against 5.85 ms (-30%)**, 3.95 ms with 16 / 32 / 48 / 64 / 96 / 128. That is gate/up (27% of the 15k prompt's kernel time) -0.8 s, the IQ4_NL down kernel similar in kind (11%): about -1.0 s of 12.7 s. Needs: the device planner (`plan_scan` / `plan_fill`) to class the groups by width, one launch a width with its own record layout (3-5 launches a layer), `shadow_moe_q` and the fp16 kernels instantiated at 16 and 128, and the DLL rebuilt. Combines with layer-wide groups (STATUS 59): fewer, fuller groups. **Kernel changes tried on the bench, bit-identical, none kept:** the quantized weights' blocks staged in shared memory with `cp.async` one K unit ahead (the decoders' loads keep their alignment by copying with the row's offset mod 4): IQ3_XXS 7.9 -> 7.2 ms (+9%), IQ2_S 7.5 -> 8.1 (-8%), IQ3_S 7.2 -> 7.5 (-4%), IQ4_NL 6.5 -> 7.0 (-7%) (registers 70 -> 91 and 27 KB of shared memory a block cost occupancy and L1); the activations' loads issued before the weights' decode: IQ3_XXS 7.9 -> 6.8 (-14%), IQ2_S 7.5 -> 7.3 (-3%), IQ3_S +2%, IQ4_NL +2%, IQ3_XXS in groups of 64 +7%: about 1% of the prompt's kernel time, a tie. `kernels/matvec.cu` is as committed. In the engine (Nsight, 9.6k prompt) the staged kernel read -10% / +7% / +2% / +6% on the same four, matching the bench. **Also:** an Nsight window of two prompts sent back to back is one window (`tools/nsys_last_run.py` splits on 3 s gaps), so launch counts are doubled; profile one prompt. **Next:** the width-bucketed planner (predicted -1.0 s at 15k, a day of kernel and planner work with the exact / quality / needle gates), or Phase 1.

- **2026-11-08 (62): fix for STATUS 61 - the width-class planner failed on a small row buffer ("internal error: width-class plan: 3274176 bytes of tables for 2949120 (768 rows)", the conversation's cache reset); found from a screenshot of the launcher.** **Cause:** `bucket_layout` has a part that does not shrink with the rows (one group an expert and class: 3 parts x 512 x (8 + 4 (1 + 2 W)) bytes for W = 16 / 32 and 632 groups for 64, twice for the two phases: ~3.2 MB), but `bb.gtab` was sized 96 k words a row (3,840 bytes a row at k = 10), so it held the tables only from ~850 rows up; STATUS 61's tests all ran 2,048-row buffers. A prompt whose batch buffer is smaller (the launcher's session had 768 rows) failed in every layer, and the assert turned it into a 400 with a cache reset. **Reproduced** on the STATUS 61 build with `SHADOW_SUB_ROWS=768`: prompts of 1.9k, 6.9k and 13.5k tokens all fail (the tail chunks of 356, 419 and 768 rows over). **Fix** (`session.rs`): `gtab` gets `max(96 k, tables / (4 rows))` words a row, `bb_table_extra` adds the difference to `big_batch_bytes` and to the KV lend's row choice and size (`lend_kv`), and `moe_staged` takes the planner only when `bucket_layout(m).total <= gtab.bytes` (else the fixed-width device planner), so no pass can fail on it. **Checked** (fixed build, fixed prompts of 1,892 / 6,930 / 13,475 tokens, greedy 40 tokens, planner on / off, hashes of the reply): `SHADOW_SUB_ROWS=768` e8c200f0 b13ea75b 2b8b5285 on and off; 512 rows (2 prompts) 7d8be55e c355f509 on and off; default c6e36f36 56649480 006cf390 on and off: **byte-identical every time**, and the speed on / off is 379 / 367, 1,101 / 1,046, 1,240 / 1,168 tok/s at 768 rows, 378 / 384, 1,227 / 1,170, 1,382 / 1,306 at the default (one server each, inside the noise). Gates as in STATUS 61: `--exact` hash 162a8e07 (two prompts) and `quality_test` 12 of 12; `cargo test --release --workspace` 35 test binaries ok; needle 15k 8/8, needle 20k `multi` 7/8 (one MAPLE item at 0.66; STATUS 58 and 61 had one miss too), `prefix_restore_test` 3/3. **Not caused by the planner, not looked into:** `SHADOW_SUB_ROWS=256` (the smallest value the setting takes) fails with CUDA_ERROR_ILLEGAL_ADDRESS on the first prompt with the planner off too, on the STATUS 61 build and on this one. **Lesson:** every experiment that sizes a buffer per row needs a run at the smallest row count the engine can use, not only the default one (`SHADOW_SUB_ROWS=512` / `768` is the cheap way to get one).
