- **2026-11-08 (67): a regression of STATUS 66 found and fixed (the grouped embedding upload ran out of VRAM on a 20k-token prompt), and the DeltaNet kernel with three value heads a thread: bit-identical, 1.08x standalone, ~0.3% of a 15k prompt.** **The regression:** STATUS 66's grouped embedding rows allocated a 5 MB device scratch (`ctx.alloc`) at the start of every long pass. With the needle 20k `multi` prompt at 20,252 tokens (STATUS.md had grown by ~50 tokens since STATUS 66's gate run, 20,202 before) the big batch had taken nearly all of the VRAM and the allocation failed: `cuMemcpyHtoDAsync failed (2)` = CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY, HTTP 400 "internal error", the session reset (a second prompt after it died the same way: `prefix_restore_test` also failed). **4 of 4 runs failed; with `SHADOW_DELTA_KH=0` (the new DeltaNet kernel off) and with `SHADOW_KEEP_PIPE=0` they still failed; with `SHADOW_EMBED_ROWS=0` the same gate passed 8/8 and 3/3**, which put it on the grouped embedding. **It had been shipped:** the launcher build of 2026-11-08 10:35 contains it (no user report; a prompt of about 20k tokens, or any pass that leaves the VRAM this full, would have hit it). **Fix:** the group's rows go through `bb.rn` (the normalized rows, not written before layer 0; `g_rows` = its size in rows, at most 512; fewer than 64 rows of it or `SHADOW_EMBED_ROWS=0` keep the per-token loop): no allocation and no synchronize at the end of the block. Checked: needle 20k `multi` 8/8 and prefix restore 3/3 with no OOM line; reply hashes of three fixed prompts (1,895 / 6,927 / 13,490 tokens) identical with the grouped embedding on and off at the default (cd11a1ca a92cccf4 3ddc5853) and with `SHADOW_SUB_ROWS=768` (492f7f37 2f1099d4 b300523f). **Lesson:** nothing may allocate on the device at the start of a pass (the VRAM is full by design: ~350 MiB free while serving); STATUS 66's gates had passed only because the prompts were 50 tokens shorter. **DeltaNet (`delta_steps128_kh`, `_kh2`, `_kh1` in `kernels/forward.cu`, harness `bench/delta_bench.cu`, `bench/mmabench/build_delta.bat`):** the three value heads of a key head (heads qh, qh + 16, qh + 32) in one thread with each q / k float4 serving all three, 8 tokens' q, k, v, b and a copied ahead by `cp.async` (Strata 0.1.44's `gdn_rec_kh_kernel` idea, read in STATUS 63), the sums as shuffles inside the warp (the four row quarters of a column are four lanes), three `__syncthreads` per block of 8 tokens instead of two per token; the arithmetic of `delta_steps128_f4` in the same order, so the same bits. **Bit-identical to f4** (outputs and state after, 12.6 M words and 786 k words) at m = 2,048, 2,045, 2,041, 8, 5, 1 for all three block shapes. **Timings (48 heads, one sub-chunk, per layer, the 5070's 48 SMs):** m 2,048: f4 1.709 ms, kh (4 warps, 64 blocks) 1.611 (1.06x), **kh2 (2 warps, 128 blocks) 1.588 (1.08x)**, kh1 (1 warp, 256 blocks) 2.221 (0.77x); m 512: 0.435 / 0.401 / 0.399 / 0.564. In the engine (`SHADOW_DELTA_KH=0` keeps f4): kh2 for prompt passes with 3 value heads a key head and head dimension 128; reply hashes identical on and off (cd11a1ca a92cccf4 3ddc5853). **It is worth ~0.3% of a 15k prompt** (0.12 ms x ~36 layers x 7.4 sub-chunks = ~30 ms): below what an A-B-B-A shows, so no engine speed claim; a tie or better goes default. **Why not more:** the kernel is near the FP32 issue limit (~4 operations an element: decay multiply, k-dot fma, update fma, q-dot fma; 128 FP32 lanes an SM; the measured 0.78 us a token is ~60-70% of that bound), so only fewer operations help; Strata's formulation (the k-dot before the decay, the update as `fmaf(g, s, k * delta)`) needs 3 and would be ~25% fewer, but rounds differently (not bit-identical to the float reference; untried). **Gates (fixed build):** `--exact` hash 162a8e07 (two prompts) and `quality_test` 12 of 12; `cargo test --release --workspace` 35 test binaries ok (the drift test knows the three new entry points); needle 15k 8/8, needle 20k `multi` **8/8** (the MAPLE item of STATUS 58-66 answers right now: the prompt text moved by 50 tokens), `prefix_restore_test` 3/3.
- **2026-11-08 (68): Nsight Compute on the grouped MoE kernels (elevated, `bench/ncu_moe_now.ps1`, report `bench/ncu_moe_down.ncu-repz`): latency-bound on the weight decode, not on the tensor cores or DRAM.** The 9.7k-token prompt, engine on the 4 GiB tier, 12 launches of `mma_grouped{16,,64}_{iq2_s,q2_0}` (the fused `_gu_` kernels never launched with this model: that profile matched nothing and wrote no report). The six full-size launches of one layer (A = iq2_s, B = q2_0; classes 16 / 32 / 64): A 1.34 / 1.03 / 2.80 ms, B 0.50 / 0.46 / 1.35 ms. **Every one of them looks alike:** SM throughput 43-55%, DRAM 14-22%, L2 hit 93-94%, **issue slots busy 28%**, IPC 1.1 of 4, **no eligible warp in 71% of cycles** (0.38 eligible a scheduler), 17 cycles a warp between issues, of which 57-60% waiting on a global/L1TEX load; occupancy 41% (72-76 registers, 18.4 KB static shared memory: 5-7 blocks of 4 warps an SM). The tensor pipe and the DRAM are idle most of the time. **Where the stalls sit (iq2_s, 64-class, 330k samples):** ~40% on nine `SHF.R` shifts that consume the result of `LDG.E.64.CONSTANT` (the codebook gather: 18.3 M sectors a load, **13.3 M of them excess**, 73%) plus the `LOP3` and `LDS` after it; the weight tile itself comes in as clean `LDG.E.128` (0 excess), but the per-block scale / high-bit bytes arrive as `LD.E.U8` / `LD.E.U16` (2.5-3.0 M sectors each, 75% excess), and the 'uncoalesced' warning is 46% of all sectors (est. speedup 45%). Shared memory has no bank conflicts. **Reading:** the decode is a chain of dependent global loads (byte → grid index → 8-byte gather → shifts → pack) with too few warps to hide it; it is neither FLOP- nor bandwidth-limited. **Candidates, in the order of expected gain:** (1) stage the 2 KB-8 KB codebook in shared memory (or keep it in registers per lane) and read the scale / high-bit bytes as one coalesced 16-byte load per lane, then decode from registers; (2) deeper `cp.async` prefetch of the next weight tile so the decode of tile t overlaps the load of t+1; (3) cut registers below 64 for 8 blocks an SM. All three are testable in the standalone harness (`bench/mma_bench.cu`, seconds a run) and keep the arithmetic, so output stays bit-identical. Not started.
- **2026-11-08 (69): the grouped MoE kernels fetch the next weight tile while this one multiplies (bit-identical; -3..-29% a launch in the harness, ~0.8% of a 15k pass in the engine; default).** STATUS 68's reading (the decode is a chain of dependent global loads with 20 warps an SM to hide it) led to a restructure of `mma_core` in `kernels/matvec.cu` for formats with a `DecRaw` entry: the per-thread bytes of tile t+1 are loaded into registers (`load`) right after tile t is decoded and stored, so they arrive during t's matrix multiplies, and the decode (`dec8`) runs on them at the top of the next round; where `deep(TOK)` the codebook gather (`gather`: the IQ2S / IQ3XXS grids) is one tile ahead too (two tiles of raw bytes in flight). The arithmetic is `Dec8`'s, operation for operation, so the output hash is the same. **Harness (`bench/mma_bench.cu`, `bench/mmabench/build.bat`, 2,048 tokens, ragged counts, launch ms head -> new):** `q2_0` 16/32/64-token groups 6.38 -> 5.13 (-19.5%), 6.24 -> 4.42 (-29.3%), 6.21 -> 5.21 (-16.0%); `iq2_s` 9.41 -> 9.06 (-3.7%), 7.56 -> 6.59 (-12.8%), 7.57 -> 7.06 (-6.8%); `iq3_xxs` 16: tie (left on the old path), 7.94 -> 6.69 (-15.8%), 7.20 -> 6.34 (-11.9%); `iq4_nl` 16: 8.81 -> 8.37 (-4.9%), 32 and 64 left on the old path; `iq3_s` left on the old path in all three. **The first try applied it to every class and lost where it cost registers** (a three-word raw value: `iq3_s` 64 +21%, `iq4_nl` 64 +6.6%, `iq3_xxs` 16 +5%, `q2_0` 64 +1% - 72 -> 79-95 registers drops blocks an SM from 7 toward 5-6, and shared memory already caps it at 5); `DecRaw<Q>::use(TOK)` now turns the path on only where it measured faster, the raw value is two words for `q2_0` / `iq2_s` and three for the others. **Tried and dropped:** `__restrict__` on the weight pointer (the SASS showed generic `LD.E` loads; no change, 7.55 -> 7.56 ms) and the IQ2S codebook in shared memory (8 KB a block: 7.31 -> 7.80 ms, worse - 3 blocks an SM instead of 5). **Engine, kernel time (Nsight Systems, `--cuda-graph-trace=node`, 15k prompt, per-launch averages new / committed engine):** `mma_grouped64_iq3_xxs` 2,392 / 2,647 us (-9.6%, x244 launches = ~62 ms), `mma_grouped64_iq2_s` 2,648 / 2,778 (-4.7%, ~37 ms), the 16-token and `iq4_nl` kernels within +-1% (their harness gains do not show with the engine's group fills): **about 0.8% of the pass's kernel time** (~100 ms of ~12 s); in the A-B-B-A (STATUS-style: 4 + 4 fresh servers, first version of the change) 9.6k 1,375 / 1,373 tok/s, 15k 1,448 / 1,412 - a tie inside the +-8% wall-clock noise. **Gates:** reply hashes on the three fixed prompts identical to the committed engine (cd11a1ca a92cccf4 3ddc5853); `--exact` hash 162a8e07 on two prompts; `quality_test` 12 of 12; `cargo test --release --workspace` 35 test binaries ok; needle 15k 8/8, needle 20k `multi` 8/8, `prefix_restore_test` 3/3. No VRAM or RAM change; decode kernels untouched. **Default on (a tie or better, bit-identical); no switch** (a revert is the one `use()` line). Not converted: the 48-token class (`mma_grouped48_*`, unused by the planner), the fused `_gu_` kernels (never launched with this model), `Q6_K` / `IQ4_XS` / `Q5_K` / `Q4_K` experts (other models), and the dense `mma_cols` kernels for the non-MMQ types. **What is left in these kernels:** still 41% occupancy and 28% issue slots busy; the next lever is registers (<64 for 8 blocks) or a layer-wide group that raises the tokens per weight tile, not the decode.
- **2026-11-08 (70): a server hang found from the user's chat, reproduced, and fixed: a shared-prefix snapshot point a few tokens into the unread prompt ended the VRAM-cache lend and sent the rest of the prompt through the non-staged path in one huge chunk.** The launcher's server sat at "Reading your message: 3 / 17,626 tokens" for minutes with the GPU at 95-97% and only 73 W (telemetry: `prefill_done` 3, `busy` true, `queued` 1). **Not the 2026-11-08 kernel change:** the build from before STATUS 69 hangs the same way, as does `--dflash off`. **Reproduction:** the chat is in the page's LevelDB (`shadow_chats_v1`); `buildContextMessages` extracted from `ui.rs` and run in Node with the server's `/v1/tools` rebuilds the requests; the last request alone prefills fine (20k tokens in ~15 s), **the last three in order on one server hang at the third: `prefill_done` 6 of 16,119** (the first two also carry the conversation's one picture and the server-side compaction). **Cause (`engine.rs`, the prompt loop):** `snap_at` (where the shared-prefix snapshot goes) was 6 tokens past what the restored snapshot already held, so the first chunk was `end = at` = 6 tokens; the lend-ending rule just below it (`lent && end - i <= MAX_VERIFY` -> `restore_cache()`, meant for the tail of a prompt) fired on that 6-token chunk, `lent` became false, and the next chunk, still sized for the lend (`chunk` = `batch_rows`, ~14-16k), ran on the non-staged path, which needs a cache slot in every layer for every routed expert and is built for a handful of tokens: minutes of misses at full GPU load, not an infinite loop (the 200 s curl timeout passed with 6 of 16,119 and the GPU at 96-99%). It needs the snapshot point to land 1-8 tokens past the cached prefix, which a normal conversation does now and then (here: after the compacted rolling context shifted the shared start). **Fix:** a snapshot split that would leave a chunk of <= MAX_VERIFY tokens is skipped while the lend is on (the snapshot is only a start for the next new conversation). **Check:** the same three requests in order on the fixed build: 14,671, 23,382 and 16,119 tokens read, the third in ~20 s (1,300 tok/s), replies normal (tool calls). Gates on the fixed build below. **Not changed, for the record:** a prompt whose last chunk is tiny still ends the lend and reads that tail non-staged (right: it is a handful of tokens); the first chunk after a restore of the conversation snapshot is unaffected.
  **Second finding of the same day (gates after the fix): the 20k-token gates died with `CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` (at a `cuMemcpyHtoDAsync` / `cuMemsetD8`, HTTP 400, session reset), on the committed STATUS 69 build and on the build with the hang fix, but not on the 13:50 build (`needle 20k multi` 8/8, `prefix_restore_test` 3/3) - nor on the STATUS 69 source with the new decode path switched off (`use()` false for every format).** The engine leaves ~100 MiB of VRAM free by design; the driver's **lazy module loading** takes device memory for a kernel's code when the kernel first launches, which on a 20k prompt is in the middle of the pass; STATUS 69's kernels added ~43 KB of code (cubin 4,454,586 -> 4,497,274 bytes; no register spills, no stack frames in any of them), enough to push that load over the edge in some runs (the same source had passed the gates an hour earlier: a margin of a few KB, not a deterministic failure - the same signature as the grouped embedding's scratch in STATUS 67). **Fix:** `driver.rs` sets `CUDA_MODULE_LOADING=EAGER` before `cuInit` unless the user set it (every kernel's code is loaded with its module, at start-up, where the expert cache is sized after it). Measured: the committed build with `CUDA_MODULE_LOADING=EAGER` passes `needle 20k multi` 8/8 and `prefix_restore_test` 3/3; the expert cache is **1,843 slots against 1,845** (2 slots, 4 MiB: 0.1%), `ready in` unchanged (36 s). It also removes this class of failure for the next kernel that grows. Rule for the future: **never leave the margin to the driver** - allocation at pass start (STATUS 67) and first-launch module loads are the same hazard.
- **2026-11-08 (71): "it did not complete" in the chat page was the page's own 14-step cap ending the turn silently, not the engine.** After the STATUS 70 restart the user's chat (a code-reading task: many small `Get-Content` reads) showed the last tool outputs, "Ready", GPU 0% and "Completed in 283.22s", with no final answer. The server log had normal completions and no error; the stored chat (page LevelDB, `shadow_chats_v1`) shows **exactly 14 assistant turns since the last user message** (16 tool results) and a history ending on tool results: `MAX_AGENT_TURNS = 14` in `ui.rs` ended the loop with no message. Fix (page only): when the loop ends at the cap and the last history entry is a tool result, a note bubble says "Stopped after 14 steps (the limit for one message) without a final answer. Send "continue" ..." and the timing line reads "Stopped at the 14-step limit after N s"; history is untouched, so "continue" resumes from the tool results. Not changed: the cap itself (14), which a code-reading task of this kind reaches easily; the rolling context keeps the payload bounded, so a higher cap or a forced final answer on the last step are options for the user to choose. JS syntax checked (`node --check` on the page script); no engine change.
  **Amended the same day at the user's request ("can there not be several more steps? or unlimited?"): the cap is gone, replaced by a stuck-guard.** `MAX_AGENT_TURNS` is removed (`while (true)`); a message may use any number of steps, and Stop still ends it. The request stays bounded as before: the page's `buildContextMessages` shortens older tool outputs to previews and folds old user turns, and the server's `fit_context` compacts on its own (a 92-message / 168k-character request, one user message, was reduced to 14,671 tokens and answered in the STATUS 70 replays); with a single user message in a long agent run only the tool-output previews and the server's truncation can shrink the context, so a very long run reads its oldest tool outputs as one-line previews (as designed). The one guard: the same tool call (name and arguments) in 4 consecutive turns stops the run with "Stopped: the same tool call 4 times in a row, with nothing new" (history keeps the results; "continue" resumes). Not exercised against a live model here: the page script passes `node --check` and the engine builds; the behavior is logic-only (a counter on the call signature).
- **2026-11-08 (72): a loop guard for greedy decoding (thinking and answer), found from the user's screenshot of a thinking loop.** The chat showed one sentence ("Let me look at the engine loop around lines 285-340 ...") repeated dozens of times in the think block at 28-64 tok/s; the run left the loop only at the Medium thinking budget (4,000 tokens: log `4068 tokens in 63.2 s`, `2895 of 2922 drafted tokens accepted` - the lookup drafter predicts a loop perfectly, so it ran fast and cost a minute), after which the server's closer ("That is enough thinking: the answer now.") let the model go on. Cause: greedy-only decoding (`temperature`, `top_p` are accepted and ignored, no repetition penalty) on a long, shortened history (59 tool outputs compacted to previews): the model re-plans a read it can no longer see the result of. **Guard (`engine.rs`, the decode loop, fast modes only: `spec > 0`; `--exact` untouched; `SHADOW_LOOP_GUARD=0` off):** `repeating_tail(tokens, min_reps, min_total)` finds a stretch of 8-400 tokens repeated back to back; **while thinking** every 32 tokens, >= 3 repeats over >= 96 tokens (a shorter period needs more repeats) closes the think block with "I am repeating myself, so that is enough thinking: the answer now."; **in the answer** (or a reply without thinking) every 64 tokens, >= 5 repeats over >= 240 tokens ends the reply with "[Stopped: the reply was repeating itself.]" (finish `stop`) - a higher bar because an answer may repeat on purpose (a table, similar blocks), and without it a reply that loops runs until the context is full (the page sends no `max_tokens`). A unit test covers the detector (period 35 twice is no loop, thrice is; 8 tokens x3 is none, x12 is; plain text none; the answer's bar). **Live probe** (two prompts asking the model to repeat a sentence while thinking, Medium, greedy, test server): without the answer-phase part, prompt 1 ran all 6,000 tokens in 66 s repeating "I need to verify the engine loop first." and prompt 0 1,509 tokens in 20 s; with it they stop at **1,536 tokens in 21 s** and **1,153 tokens in 16 s**, the guard's line in the log ("started repeating itself after 1526 tokens") and the note at the end of the reply. (The first version, thinking only, did not fire on these: their thinking was short and the answer looped - which is why the answer phase was added.) **Gates:** `cargo test --release --workspace` 35 test binaries ok, `--exact` hash 162a8e07, `quality_test` 12 of 12, needle 15k 8/8, needle 20k `multi` 8/8, prefix restore 3/3 (run on the thinking-only version; the answer-phase edit touches only the guard's own branch and the unit test). **Fixed-prompt hashes in the default mode (6k / 20k / 40k chars): cd11a1ca / a92cccf4 / 0f43079e, the third was 3ddc5853 before STATUS 70**: with `SHADOW_LOOP_GUARD=0` the same 0f43079e, so not the guard; with `CUDA_MODULE_LOADING=LAZY` 3ddc5853 again, so it is the **eager module loading of STATUS 70** (the expert cache is 1,843 slots against 1,845: which experts are resident decides which routed experts the fast mode's tail skip leaves out, so a different cache size changes the reply; a single 40k prompt on a fresh server gives a third hash, 2269db20: the reply depends on the cache state the earlier prompts left). Not a defect (the exact path and the quality / needle gates are unchanged), but the fixed-prompt hashes of STATUS 66-69 (cd11a1ca a92cccf4 3ddc5853) are now cd11a1ca a92cccf4 0f43079e. **A slip, for the record:** the gate run for this entry stopped the user's live server (pid 28420, restarted by them during my turn) because my scripts and the launcher both use port 8081 and my cleanup killed the listener; all my test scripts now use port 8091 and the cleanup script's ports are 8091 / 8095. **Not done:** a real repetition penalty (changes ordinary outputs, interacts with speculative verification: measure separately).
- **2026-11-09 (73): the user's long chat run ended with "the reply was cut off: the conversation has no room left" after 66 tokens (35 minutes into an unlimited-step run, STATUS 71): two causes found in its screenshot, both fixed in code, live check pending.** (1) **`read_file` ignored the line range the model asked for.** The tool's schema names `start` / `end`; the model calls `start_line` / `end_line` (and `file_path`), as other tools do, and an unknown argument was silently dropped, so every call began at line 1 and returned 400 lines (~5k tokens): the screenshot shows `read_file(start_line 2415, end_line 2530)` answering with line 1 of the file, and the model's own thinking says "the read_file tool returned the beginning of the file (lines 1-400) for all requests regardless of start". That is why the run kept re-reading the top of the same files, re-planning the same step (and, with the compaction of old outputs to previews, looped: STATUS 72) and filled the window. **Fix (`orchestrator.rs`):** `ARGUMENT_ALIASES` maps `start_line` / `line_start` / `startLine` / `from_line` -> `start`, `end_line` / `line_end` / `endLine` / `to_line` -> `end`, `file_path` / `filepath` / `file` -> `path` for `read_file` (and the path names for `edit_file` / `write_file`), our own name winning when both are given; and `execute_tool` appends "(note: ignored unknown argument(s) X; this tool's arguments are: ...)" to the result of any native tool called with an argument it does not have, so a model that guesses a name learns the right one at once. Unit test: a real file read with `start_line 3`, `end_line 4` returns lines 3-4 and no note, `lines: "3-4"` is reported. (2) **The server's compaction left the reply 66 tokens.** `fit_context` reserves 2,048 tokens for a reply, shortens older tool outputs (stage 1) and folds old user turns (stage 2) - but in one long run there is a single user message (nothing to fold) and stage 1 keeps the tool outputs of the active step whole; a batch of big reads (each 400 lines, from (1)) filled the window and stage 3 (middle-truncating the longest message, 6 passes) left no room. **Fix (`engine.rs`, stage 2b):** if the prompt is still too long after stage 2, the tool outputs of the active step are shrunk to previews as well, oldest first, the newest left whole, stopping as soon as it fits. Unit test: one user message, six 5k-token tool outputs, window 4,096: the newest stays whole, an older one is a preview, the prompt leaves the reply its room. `cargo test --release -p shadow-serve`: 20 tests ok. **Not run live:** the user's launcher server was up when this was finished (GPU in use), so the replay of the chat's long requests (STATUS 70) through the new compaction stage, and a release rebuild, wait until it is free; no kernel or decode code changed.
- **2026-11-09 (74): "the reply was cut off: the conversation has no room left" again, after 5,427 tokens: the reply reserve ignored how long this model thinks.** The user's screenshot after STATUS 73: prompt 20,165 tokens read, reply 5,427 tokens at 30.8 tok/s, 25,592 of the 25,600-token window, cut off mid-sentence in a block that reads like more thinking ("let me re-check what I actually saw: ..."). Not the STATUS 73 problem (that request was shrunk and the reply had room); here the room was 5.4k tokens and the reply used it: Medium effort allows 4,000 thinking tokens, the server then closes the think block ("That is enough thinking: the answer now.") and this model goes on thinking in the answer. `fit_context` reserved only 2,048 tokens for a reply when the request sets no `max_tokens` (the page sends none), enough for a short answer but not for budget + answer. **Fix (`engine.rs`):** with thinking on the reserve is `2,048 + the effort's thinking budget` (Low 1,000, Medium 4,000, High 8,000, XHigh 12,000; `thinking_budget()` is now shared with the decode loop), capped at a third of the window; thinking off keeps 2,048. At the 25,600 window: Medium 6,048 (a prompt may be up to 19.5k tokens, was 23.5k), High and XHigh 8,533 (17.1k). A bigger reserve means the compaction (previews of older tool outputs, STATUS 73's stage 2b) starts earlier in a long run: less old detail in exchange for a reply that fits. Unit test `a_thinking_reply_is_left_room_for_its_thinking`: a ~20k-token prompt in a 24k window is shrunk with thinking on and untouched with thinking off; `cargo test --release -p shadow-serve`: 21 tests ok. **Not run live:** the user's server held the GPU; a replay of the long chat requests on the new build (as in STATUS 73) is the check, and a release rebuild, once it is free. **What this does not fix:** a model that thinks past its budget and rambles in the answer can still use all of what is left (it is no longer a surprise cut at 5.4k, but a run of ~6k tokens), and the window itself (25,600 tokens, set by the VRAM left for the KV) is unchanged.
- **2026-11-09 (75): "looping" in the unlimited-step chat run: not one repeated call but a model that cannot hold what it read in a 25,600-token window; three nudges, no mechanism change.** The user's screenshot (stopped by them after 20.7 s of thinking) shows the model confused by its own context: "the tool results are shown as [earlier tool output (N chars): ...] ... my previous turns are being deleted ... let me take a step back". The saved chat (page LevelDB) has **107 tool calls in one user message (142 messages)**, 98 distinct: only 5 exact calls repeated (a `readpool.rs` slice 4x, `EXPERIMENT_SPEC_2026-11-04b.json` 3x, ...): the run reads one file in small slices and a spec again and again because the older outputs are previews (STATUS 71 removed the step cap, STATUS 73-74 shrink a long run's older tool outputs and keep room for the reply), so what it read three steps ago is gone; the STATUS 71 stuck-guard (4 identical calls in a row) never fires on that. **Changes (no kernel, decode or compaction-rule change):** (1) the default persona (`ui.rs`, used unless the user saved their own in Settings - "Reset Default" picks it up) now says the window is small and earlier tool outputs are shortened, so after reading something it will need it must write the facts (file, lines, exact text) in its reply before the next tool call, not read the same part twice, work in small steps and finish with an answer; assistant text is kept whole by the compaction, tool outputs are not, so notes in the reply are what survives; (2) a tool call identical to an earlier one in the same task gets "(note: this exact call was already made N time(s) earlier in this task and gives the same result; use the facts you noted, or read a different part.)" appended to its result (page-side counter on name + arguments); (3) the preview of a shortened tool output reads "[earlier tool output (N chars), shortened to save context; it began: ... - call the tool again if you need more of it]" (page `buildContextMessages` and the server's `fit_context` stages 1 and 2b) instead of a bare "[earlier tool output (N chars): ...]", which this model read as its turns being deleted. `cargo test --release -p shadow-serve`: 21 tests ok (the stage-2b test got a wider window: the longer preview text had used up its margin); page script passes `node --check`. **Not run live** (the user's server was up); whether the nudges stop the re-reading is for a long chat to show. **What this does not change:** the window (25,600 tokens, set by the VRAM left for the KV) and the fact that a task needing more than a few dozen steps of reading will lose detail; a model-written checkpoint summary every N steps would hold more than previews and is the next step if this is not enough.
- **2026-11-09 (76): a per-session log of tool outputs on the SSD and a `recall` tool, so what the compaction shortens can be read again (the user's suggestion after STATUS 75).** The page keeps every full tool output in its history, but the model only ever saw the 160-character previews of older ones, so a long run re-read the same files (STATUS 75: 107 calls in one message). **Now:** `orchestrator.rs` `toollog`: every output of a native tool longer than 280 characters is written to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Shadow\toolcache\s<start time>\<n>.txt` (a session is a server run; the newest 6 sessions are kept, older `s<digits>` folders removed at start; `SHADOW_TOOL_LOG_DIR` moves it) and starts with `(output #n)`; MCP tools and short outputs are left alone. A new native tool **`recall`** (`id`, `start`, `end`, `query`; aliases `start_line`, `end_line`, `text`, `search`, `output`, ...) reads output *n* again (lines `start`-`end`, default the first 300; or only the lines containing `query`), and with only a `query` searches every output of the session (`#n:line| text`, 60 hits at most); an unknown number is an error saying the log holds the outputs since the server started. When the conversation shortens an old output (the page's `buildContextMessages` and the server's `fit_context` stages 1 and 2b, one helper `shortened_tool_output`) the preview now names the number: `[earlier tool output #12 (N chars), shortened to save context; it began: ... - call recall with id 12 to read it again (a line range, or a text to search for)]`; an output without a number (an older chat, an MCP tool) keeps "call the tool again". The default persona tells the model about `recall` and still asks it to write down what it needs before the next call (notes in its reply survive whole). Tests: a long `read_file` is logged with its number, a short `echo` is not, `recall` by number and lines, by number and text (case-insensitive), across all outputs, an unknown number and an empty call; the preview helper (numbered, plain, malformed); `cargo test --release -p shadow-serve` 23 ok; page script `node --check`. **Not run live** (the user's server was up). **Scope, for the record:** it holds tool outputs only; the model's own earlier replies are kept whole by the compaction and old user turns become 140/160-character summary bullets, with no log behind them (a log of folded turns is the same mechanism, not built); the cost of a logged output is one file write; the first line of a long output is now `(output #n)`.

- **2026-11-09 (82): Phase A, steps A1 and A2 of docs/PLAN_RAM_TIER_AND_LEVERS_2026-11-09.md: the ~4.5 GiB of RAM beyond the tier was the host copy of every dense weight; the server no longer keeps it (3.6 GiB less at "ready", 1.4-2.3 GiB less in steady state, output unchanged).** **A1, where it was (`SHADOW_RAM_MAP=1` prints the process's working set / private bytes / pinned bytes at each loading checkpoint; `ram_audit.py` starts a test server on port 8091 under `run_capped.ps1` and tables resident memory at ready and after 3k / 15k prompts):** a server started with the launcher's flags was 4.5 GiB resident beyond the 36 GiB tier at "ready", all of it private (no file mapping). Callers that only wanted a tensor's size use the new `Model::dense_meta`. The uploaded bytes are the same bytes, so every kernel input is bit-for-bit what it was. **Measured (same machine, tier 36, launcher flags, 4 GiB floor, other apps as they were):** resident beyond the tier at ready 4.5 -> 0.9 GiB; after the first 3k prompt 5.4 (6.5 in a second run with the map on) -> 3.2; steady state after 15k prompts 5.2 (6.1) -> 3.8; available RAM at steady state 7.7 -> 9.8 GiB. Prompt speed is unchanged within the audit's resolution (3,220 tokens 5-6 s, 15,292 tokens 11 s, as before); no kernel and no GPU buffer changed, so no A-B-B-A pair was run. Load time is not made worse: the same bytes are read once, at upload instead of at load.
- **2026-11-09 (83): Phase A, step A3 of docs/PLAN_RAM_TIER_AND_LEVERS_2026-11-09.md: the RAM tier is built from 1 GiB chunks, and a watchdog gives chunks back when available RAM falls under the floor (a hog test: 36 -> 12 GiB of tier, 12 times, the server never stopped answering).** **Pinned region:** `PinnedRegion` reserves its whole address range and commits + registers it chunk by chunk (`new_chunked(ctx, len, CHUNK = 1 GiB)`; `new` is one chunk, so every other user is unchanged); the floor is checked after each chunk, so a fill that runs the PC low stops early instead of after 36 GiB. `shrink_by(bytes)` unregisters and decommits whole chunks from the end (nothing below moves). Two driver facts found by the first tests: the device addresses of separate registrations are NOT consecutive (a chunk 1 MiB on mapped 2 MiB further than `dev_ptr + offset`), and a host range across two registrations is refused by `cuMemcpyHtoD` (INVALID_VALUE). So the tier now packs experts so none crosses a chunk boundary (`PinnedRegion::fit`; the padding is under one expert, ~3-6 MiB, per GiB) and the two places that took `dev_ptr + off` use `dev_addr(off)`. **Tier:** `RamTier::trim` (after `synchronize`) shrinks the region, removes from `at` every expert not wholly inside what is left (`layer_size` per layer), and cuts the promotion ring's tail slots. **Watchdog:** `pinned::start_watchdog` (from `Tiers::from_args`; `SHADOW_RAM_WATCHDOG=0` off; no floor, no watchdog) samples available RAM every 250 ms; after two samples in a row under the floor (one dip is the file cache moving) it asks for enough whole chunks to bring the PC to floor + 1 GiB, then stays quiet for 2 s. The engine thread acts on the request at its next safe point - the start of `step` and of `batch`, where no copy from or read into the tier is pending (`ram_pressure_poll`, one atomic load otherwise) - so a request waits at most one sub-chunk of a prompt. The RAM black box (STATUS 81) shows the dip. **Tests:** `a_chunked_region_gives_its_tail_back` (GPU: 8 MiB + 4 KiB in 1 MiB chunks, `fit`, each chunk read through its own device address, shrink releases the tail and 3 whole chunks, nothing moves, what stayed copies exactly, everything can go) and `the_watchdog_gives_back_whole_chunks_only_when_under_the_floor` (the policy); `cargo test -p shadow-cuda` library + `--test pinned` pass. **Hog test (`ram_hog_test.py`, port 8091, tier 36, `--ram-floor-gib 5`, a process that takes RAM in 0.5 GiB steps whenever available RAM is over 4.4 GiB, never under 3.9 - above `run_capped`'s 3 GiB line):** every dip under 5 GiB was answered within one request (the log: "gave back 2 GiB under RAM pressure (~1,055 experts dropped; tier now 34 GiB, 17,881 experts); available RAM 3.88 -> 5.85 GiB", then 32, 30, ... 12 GiB: the hog took each freed GiB at once, which is harsher than a real program); requests in the window took 3-8 s (8 tokens of a 600-char prompt) against 3 s with the full tier, the last one 10 s with 12 GiB of tier left; nothing crashed, the lowest available RAM seen was 4.0 GiB, and the server answered correctly after the hog left. Without pressure the chunked tier fills the same (36.00 GiB, 18,935 experts, 28.5 s) and a 3.2k-token prompt takes 6 s as before; resident 0.9 -> 3.2 GiB beyond the tier, as in STATUS 82. **Not done:** the tier does not grow back when RAM returns (after the hog left, available RAM was 33.8 GiB and the tier stayed 12 GiB until the next start; re-filling a chunk is a 29 s/36 GiB read from the SSD, so a regrow needs a policy); a spike that arrives inside one long sub-chunk is acted on at its end; the telemetry page does not show the released bytes yet (`GpuSession::ram_tier_trimmed` has them). Next: A4, the tier sized from the free RAM at start, then Phase B.

Phase A is complete.** The rule: tier = (RAM available at the start less `TIER_RESERVE` = 13 GiB), whole GiB, at most `TIER_MAX` = 36 GiB (the largest tier measured so far; Phase B raises it), and none when under 2 GiB would be left of it. A unit test (`the_default_tier_follows_the_free_ram`) pins the rule: 49 -> 36, 64 -> 36, 45.5 -> 32, 30 -> 17, 14 or less -> none. **Phase A in total (STATUS 81-84):** a RAM black box (A0); the 4.5 GiB beyond the tier traced to the host copy of the dense weights and cut to 0.9 GiB at ready (A1, A2); a tier of 1 GiB chunks with a watchdog that gives chunks back under pressure, shown by a hog test (A3); sizing from the free RAM (A4). The plan's gate (3k <= 3.3 s at >= 43, no decode loss, minimum available above the floor) is met from 44 up. The ladder also shows why the top is safer than a straight subtraction suggests: the RAM lost after the server is ready (the file cache the SSD reads fill) shrinks as the tier grows - 6.1, 4.8, 3.5, 2.6, 2.2 GiB for 36 ... 45 - because fewer experts come from the file. **Default sizing (`pinned::tier_for_available`, replaces STATUS 84's "available less 13 GiB, at most 36"):** the biggest whole-GiB tier, at most `TIER_MAX` = 45, for which free RAM at the start - tier - `tier_overhead_gib(tier)` - 1.25 GiB is still above the floor; the overhead is the ladder's own table (everything the PC loses besides the tier, by the lowest point: 7.6 GiB at 36 or less, 6.3 at 40, 5.0 at 42, 3.8 at 44, 2.8 at 45, interpolated). It gives 45 from ~53 GiB free, 42 from 52, 36 from 49 (the old rule's point), 29 from 41.9 (a 12 GiB hog). The margin below the floor is the reaction time: the watchdog needs two samples 250 ms apart and the next step, so a program that takes 0.5 GiB every 0.5 s gets ~0.3 GiB under it. **Not measured / open:** one run per tier (an A-B-B-A pair only for 36); the ladder ran with the PC's usual idle programs, not with the heaviest ones open (the sizing reads the free RAM at the start, and the watchdog covers a program that starts later); the tier still does not grow back when RAM returns; 45 is the top of the ladder, not of the model (46.8 GiB of experts: the last 1.8 GiB would leave the VRAM-pinned experts' double, untested). **Next: Phase C, the int8 fused MoE (needs the user's go-ahead), or the remaining levers of the plan.**

The Chat tab does not call `dashRender`, so it kept saying Ready. - **2026-11-10 (91): the `search` tool's embedder crash found and fixed (any chunk of 511 or more tokens aborts `llama-embedding`), and a built-in keyword (BM25) search added: `search` answers in under a second with or without the embedder.** **The crash.** The user's chat agent saw `llama-embedding.exe` die (WER: `ucrtbase.dll`, 0xC0000409, 8:12 and twice at 9:22 on 11-10; reports go back to October). Replayed the tool's indexing outside the server (`replay_embed.py`: the same file scan, `chunk_text`, `wordy`, 32-text / 20,000-character batches and command line over J:\Shadow and the session's tool outputs, 5,494 chunks in 240 batches): **5 of the first 72 batches aborted**, stderr `ggml-cpu/ops.cpp:5056: GGML_ASSERT(i01 >= 0 && i01 < ne01) failed` - a lookup of a row past the end of a table. Bisected (`bisect_embed.py`): 5 of the 22 texts of one batch fail alone, the first a table in `crates/shadow-cuda/src/kernels.rs`; then by hand on plain text: **`(` x 509 passes, `(` x 511 aborts** - MiniLM (and BGE) have 512 position embeddings, the tokenizer adds [CLS] and [SEP], so a text of 511 or more WordPiece tokens indexes position 512. A chunk is cut at 40 lines / 900 characters, which is ~300 tokens of prose but 500-900 of code, where every symbol is a token. It is not memory (the commit limit was 113.8 GiB with 31.4 in use), not the Dashboard, and not triggered by a long input as such (over 2,048 tokens is a clean `exceeds batch size` exit 1). **What it cost:** `build` returned at the first failed batch (`embed_source(...)?`), so the background index of any code-heavy folder never finished ("the last refresh of the index failed"), and `search` itself returned the error whenever the query embedding or the 40 outputs it embeds on every call failed. **The fix (`semsearch.rs`).** (1) Token budget: `est_tokens` (a run of letters/digits ceil(len/3), every other non-space character 1: over-counts); `chunk_text` stops a chunk at 340 estimated tokens (dense code gives more, smaller chunks), and `Llama::embed` cuts every text to 400 before it goes on the command line (queries too) - both well under 510. (2) Failure isolation: `embed_tolerant` halves a failing batch until the text that fails is alone, retries that text cut in half, then gives it up (no vector, counted); after 3 batches in a row with no vector at all the embedder is switched off for the process (`embed_off`) instead of spawning crashing processes every 30 s; a source that failed is not retried in this process. (3) **BM25:** `terms` (a run of letters, digits and `_` whole, plus the parts of an identifier - `SHADOW_FUSED_MOE` -> `shadow_fused_moe`, `shadow`, `fused`, `moe`; split at `_`, at lower-to-UPPER and at letter-digit; plural `s` dropped; stop words dropped; the source's path included), per-chunk term counts computed when a chunk is made (not stored on disk), `keyword` = BM25 (k1 1.2, b 0.75), `hybrid` = reciprocal-rank fusion (k 60) of the keyword and the meaning ranking, a chunk with no vector still found by its words, a chunk with no word in common still found by meaning. (4) `build` in two phases: the text of everything new or changed goes into the index first (seconds: keyword search works from then on, `Progress::text_ready`), the vectors follow file by file; chunks unchanged keep their old vectors; a chunk without a vector is saved as zeros and read back as none (the index format is unchanged: old index files load). `add_outputs` adds a session's tool outputs as text at once and `embed_outputs` gives 40 of their chunks vectors per call. (5) `tool`: no embedder found, or it failing, is no longer an error - `(keyword results only: the embedder was not found / failed (...) / failed several times in a row and was switched off until Shadow restarts)`, `(meaning search covers N of M chunks so far; the rest are found by keywords only)`; the hits say how they matched (`score keywords 19.9, meaning 0.61`). The tool's description to the model (`orchestrator.rs`) now asks for distinctive words (names, flags, identifiers) as well as a sentence. **Checked.** 7 new tests (identifiers whole and by parts; BM25 ranking and path filter with no embedder; fusion with a vectorless chunk; the token budget; a poisoned text costs only its own vector and a build goes on past it; an embedder that always fails is switched off and the tool goes on by keywords, with vectorless chunks surviving save/load) and the old ones adapted: `cargo test -p shadow-serve --lib` 39 passed; **`real_embedder_survives_dense_code` (ignored, run by hand, 186 s): the real `llama-embedding` over `kernels.rs`, `pinned.rs`, `quants.cuh`, `iq_tables.cuh`, `matvec.cu` - every chunk got a vector, 0 failures, no switch-off.** Live on a test server (port 8091, an empty isolated cache, working folder J:\Shadow, 214 changed files / 4,854 chunks): the first `search` took 2.2 s including the reading of the files, later ones 0.4-0.5 s, the hits sensible (`SHADOW_FUSED_MOE` found in the doc comment and in `fused.rs`; `RAM tier watchdog gives chunks back` found `session.rs` and `pinned.rs`); the meaning index filled at ~2 chunks a second with 6 threads (1,179 of 4,854 in 549 s; the default is 2 threads: slower) with **0 new `llama-embedding` crashes** in the event log over that time. **Not done / open:** no quality comparison of the fused ranking against meaning-only (four queries by eye); `est_tokens` is an estimate - a text it under-counts is caught by the halve-and-retry and costs one crashed process (a WER entry) before it is given up; the persona text in `ui.rs` still says search is `by meaning` (the other agent's uncommitted file); the user's existing `semindex-*.bin` files are kept as they are (their chunks are the old, uncut ones, with vectors: they keep working, files that never finished are indexed on the next scan). `semsearch.rs` was already carrying the chat agent's uncommitted `rustfmt` pass (formatting only, checked by formatting HEAD and comparing), so this commit's diff of that file is large.
- **2026-11-10 (92): Avalon CPU-experts prompt port (`docs/EXPERIMENT_SPEC_2026-11-10.json`, Task 2 of `2026-11-04b`): closed via a zero-cost oracle bound (`SHADOW_AVALON_ORACLE=N`) and a per-layer router-sync probe (`SHADOW_AVALON_ORACLE_SYNC=1`) - N=3 is +0.11% to +0.65% even for a clairvoyant 0-cost oracle, and waiting for router tops on the host loses -5.31% prompt / -6.22% combined.** **Why the premise changed since Nov 1:** when `docs/EXPERIMENT_SPEC_2026-11-01.json` was written, prompt reading was ~130-226 tok/s and host-planned; after 8k-16k passes with 2,048-row sub-chunks, the width-class device planner (`dev_plan_buckets`, STATUS 61) and the fused int8 MMA experts (STATUS 88-89), baseline prompt reading on `bench/dfdata/prompt_heavy.json` (`--ctx 16384 --ram-tier-gib 36 --ram-floor-gib 3`) is **1,037-1,140 tok/s**, and the host never waits for the router (`tops` is `None`: `stage_copy(l + 1)` and `stage_pipe_read(l + 2)` are queued at the start of layer `l` before layer `l + 1`'s router runs on the GPU). **The oracle (`SHADOW_AVALON_ORACLE=n`, `--avalon-oracle n`, timing only, output wrong; off by default):** `stage_pipe_read` and `stage_copy` leave the `n` highest-numbered non-resident experts of every layer out of both the file read-ahead and the staged H2D DMA (`moe_staged` zeros their stage slots once at the start of a pass so the fused int8 kernels read finite zeros; stale bytes from another layer's quant crashed with CUDA 700), giving the exact upper bound of a CPU path that computes `n` cache-miss experts per layer in 0.000 ms using 0 bytes of DRAM bandwidth and no host join. **Suite 1 (`bench/avalon_oracle_prompt`, 5 arms x 3 interleaved fresh-server runs, `bench/avalon-2026-11-10/summary.md`):** on the clean single-pass warm turns (Turns 1-3: 5,013 + 3,756 + 4,298 = 13,067 tokens/run, before cumulative history exceeds 16k and triggers `fit_context` compaction), prompt throughput follows the exact DMA byte fraction removed (`n / 474`): `oracle0` 1,018.30 tok/s (1,032.10 on warm runs 1-2; `baseA`/`baseB` below 1,037.21), **`oracle3` 1,038.36 (+0.11% vs warm baseline, +0.65% on Turns 1-2: 1,116.79 vs 1,109.57 - inside the 0.8% noise floor)**, `oracle12` 1,050.21 (+1.25%), `oracle25` 1,068.47 (+3.01%), `oracle50` 1,105.32 (+6.57%). (Across all 5 warm turns including the compacted 15,678-token Turns 4-5: `oracle0` 1,140.41, `oracle3` 1,195.60, `oracle12` 1,178.82, `oracle25` 1,199.32, `oracle50` 1,249.56 - on two-pass 15.6k turns, zeroing `n > 0` experts perturbs hidden states across 48 layers and alters downstream routing density.) **Suite 2 (`bench/avalon_oracle_sync`, 3 arms x 2 interleaved fresh-server runs, Turns 1-3 = 26,134 warm prompt tokens):** `baseA` **1,037.72 tok/s** (1,040.2, 1,035.2; combined 957.70), `baseB` A/A control **1,036.69 tok/s** (1,037.8, 1,035.6; **-0.10%** prompt, -0.05% combined), and **`oracle3_sync` (`--avalon-oracle 3 --avalon-oracle-sync`, which records and synchronizes an event at `moe_staged` entry so the host could read layer `l`'s router tops before submitting a CPU job) 982.61 tok/s (972.9, 992.5; -5.31% prompt, combined 898.17 = -6.22%)**. **Verdict:** (1) at `N = 1..3` experts/layer, even a clairvoyant 0-cost oracle saves at most 0.63% of the staged copy (+0.11% to +0.65%, a tie); (2) learning which experts layer `l` routed to on the host breaks the device planner's host-ahead overlap and costs ~4.5 ms/layer (-5.31%) before any CPU compute or DRAM read occurs; (3) clearing 2% even without sync would require 15-25 experts/layer, which at prompt batch sizes (~32 tokens/expert = 3.06-4.36 ms/expert on 14 AVX-512 threads, STATUS 2026-11-02) takes 46-109 ms of CPU compute per layer against a ~12 ms resident-expert window while halving PCIe DMA bandwidth on the shared 30 GB/s DDR4-2933 bus (`h2d_probe.rs`). Full port not built; the oracle hooks stay gated and off by default.
- **2026-11-10 (93): Prompt path fixed-cost & fused-planner optimizations (`SHADOW_FUSED_FAST_PLAN`, startup pre-allocated & retained `StagePipe` & `staging`, batched `restore_cache` DMA, parallel `embed_rows`, and honoring `"cache_prompt": false`; `--no-prompt-opt` disables all five): +9.7% on first 3k prompt (3.42 -> 3.12 s, 919 tok/s), +4.0% on warm 3k (2.72 -> 2.62 s, 1,001 tok/s), +2.8% at 9.6k (5.89 -> 5.73 s, 1,689 tok/s), +1.4% at 15k (8.70 -> 8.57 s, 1,754 tok/s - 15.5% faster than Strata 0.2.11's 9.90 s), bit-identical replies.** **Where the remaining prompt time was (`SHADOW_PHASES=1` at tier 45, floor 3.5):** (1) At tier 45 (`54.2 GiB` available before fill -> `~5.5 GiB` free after pinning the 45 GiB tier), `restore_cache` still had an old `available_ram() >= ram_floor() + 4 GiB` (`7.5 GiB`) retention guard from before the chunked RAM tier and watchdog existed, which was **always false** at tier 45! Consequently, the server dropped its three `StagePipe` read-ahead buffers (`546 MiB` pinned at tier 45) at the end of *every single prompt* (`60-68 ms` in `cuMemHostUnregister` inside `restore_cache`) and re-allocated + pinned them at the start of *every prompt* (`70-90 ms` in `stage_pipe_init`), plus allocated the 256-slot `staging` buffer (`502 MiB`, `137 ms`) on the first prompt (`3k_a`). Pre-allocating `staging` and `sp_spare` in `GpuSession::with_tiers` and removing the redundant `restore_cache` retention guard (since `PinnedRegion::new` checks `ram_floor()` at creation and the chunked tier watchdog `pinned::start_watchdog` logs a warning and releases tier chunks if available RAM ever dips below `ram_floor()`) cuts `lend_cache` from `316 ms -> 91 ms` on `3k_a`, `164 ms -> 106 ms` on `3k_b`, `124 ms -> 55 ms` on `9k6`, and `113 ms -> 45 ms` on `15k`, while eliminating the `55-68 ms` drop in `restore_cache` (`259 ms -> 205 ms` on 3k, `220 ms -> 171 ms` on 15k). (2) **Fused-expert fast device planning (`SHADOW_FUSED_FAST_PLAN=1`, default on; `=0` or `--no-fused-fast-plan` off):** now that `shadow_fused.dll` covers all 48 MoE layers (STATUS 88-89), `dev_plan_buckets` was still zeroing `7.6 MB` of width-class record tables per sub-chunk (`2.9 GB` of GPU memset per 15k prompt), launching `plan_scan_w` and `plan_fill_w` (`768` launches per 15k prompt), uploading 3 parts of `dp_eaddr` (12 KB) + `dp_lay`, and running `fu.quantize` twice per sub-chunk when `any_res` was true. When `fused_covers(m, infos)` holds, `dev_plan_buckets` now uploads only part 0 of `dp_eaddr` (4 KB), skips `zero_words`, `plan_scan_w`, and `plan_fill_w` (keeping only `route_topk_rows` and `fill_words`), quantizes `bb.u` once in `fused_experts`, and bypasses `fu.mask` (`ids_in = dp_tidx.ptr`) when `ph == 1 && !any_res`. (3) **Batched DMA in `restore_cache` (`SHADOW_RESTORE_SINGLE=1` off):** replaces `1,775-1,809` single `cuMemcpyHtoDAsync` calls with one `cuMemcpyBatchAsync` batch upload. (4) **Parallel host `embed_rows` (`SHADOW_EMBED_PAR=0` off):** parallelizes the `Q6_K` host token-embedding dequantization in `batch()` across `std::thread::scope` chunks (`21 -> 16 ms` at 3k, `75 -> 67 ms` at 15k). (5) **Honor `"cache_prompt": false` (`crates/shadow-serve/src/lib.rs`, `engine.rs`) & remove leftover hard RAM guards:** `/v1/chat/completions` now parses `"cache_prompt"` (default `true` so normal chat/agent turns keep full prefix and conversation snapshots) and skips taking host snapshots (`sess.snapshot_to(true, true)`) when `"cache_prompt": false` is passed (`SHADOW_FORCE_CONV_SNAP=1` forces them); `expand_pictures` now logs a warning (`shadow serve: warning: encoding a picture with ... GiB free`) instead of returning an error if free RAM is still below `ram_floor() + 1.8 GiB` after `answer()`'s tier release, and `chat()`'s conversation-snapshot check uses the configured `ram_floor()` instead of the hardcoded `RAM_FLOOR + 3 GiB`. **Also tested and reverted (`right_size_lend`):** sizing `want` in `lend_cache` to `prompt_hint`'s `rparts` (lending `1,026` of `1,843` slots instead of `1,825` on a 3k prompt, Round 1 `bench/prompt-opt-2026-11-10/results.json`) was a loss (`3k_b` `2.72 -> 2.75 s`) and changed `3k_b`'s reply hash (`4ee10896 -> 23de2ec5`) because `slot_offset` is ordered by layer (`0..48`): lending the tail left 100% of layers `0..20` resident (`any_res == true`, forcing two-phase `fused_experts` launches) and 0% of layers `22..47` resident while saving only 7.4% of the H2D copy. Reverted so `lend_cache` always lends the full `want`. **Measured (interleaved A-B-B-A on fresh servers, `--ctx 24576 --ram-tier-gib 45 --ram-floor-gib 3.5`, `tools/ab_prompt_opt.py`, `bench/prompt-opt-2026-11-10/results_r2.json`):** `3k_a` (2,867 tok, first prompt): `base` 3.43, 3.41 s (mean **3.42 s, 837.9 tok/s**), `opt` 3.13, 3.11 s (mean **3.12 s, 919.1 tok/s, +9.7%**), hash `b8b75e04` on all 4; `3k_b` (2,622 tok, warm prompt): `base` 2.73, 2.72 s (mean **2.72 s, 962.2 tok/s**), `opt` 2.63, 2.61 s (mean **2.62 s, 1,000.9 tok/s, +4.0%**), hash `4ee10896` on all 4; `9k6` (9,673 tok): `base` 5.86, 5.91 s (mean **5.89 s, 1,642.9 tok/s**), `opt` 5.72, 5.74 s (mean **5.73 s, 1,689.1 tok/s, +2.8%**), hash `9034540f` on all 4; `15k` (15,034 tok): `base` 8.69, 8.70 s (mean **8.70 s, 1,728.7 tok/s**), `opt` 8.54, 8.60 s (mean **8.57 s, 1,753.6 tok/s, +1.4%**), hash `f095db85` on all 4; `decode200` (200 tok): `base` **53.2 tok/s**, `opt` **52.9 tok/s**, hash `46597785` on all 4. **Where Shadow stands vs Strata 0.2.11 (`docs/PLAN_BEAT_STRATA_2026-11-07.md`):** Shadow now beats Strata on **decode** (**52.9 vs 47.7 tok/s, +10.9%**) and **15k prompts** (**8.57 s / 1,754 tok/s vs Strata's 9.90 s / 1,543 tok/s, +13.6% faster**), is within 4% at **9.6k** (**5.73 s / 1,689 tok/s vs Strata's 5.50 s**), and reads **warm 3k prompts in 2.62 s (1,001 tok/s)** vs Strata's 2.40 s (the remaining 0.22 s gap at 3k is the `91-106 ms` KV-tail remap in `lend_cache` + `205 ms` `restore_cache` to keep all 1,843 VRAM cache slots warm for 53 tok/s decode). All five optimizations are on by default under the tie-or-better rule.
- **2026-11-10 (94): Prompt-path & decode Round 3 optimizations (`0.1.35`, `SHADOW_VMM_POOL`, `SHADOW_SUBCHUNK_FAST`, `SHADOW_MMQ_REUSE`, `SHADOW_RESTORE_OVERLAP`, `SHADOW_DIRECT_TAB`, and `PinnedRegion::build` warning-only floor check; `--no-prompt-opt-r3` disables the five Round 3 switches): `lend_cache` 91 -> 40 ms at 3k (45 -> 19 ms at 15k), `restore_cache` 205 -> 166 ms at 3k (170 -> 151 ms at 15k), warm 3k `2.99 s` (`958 tok/s`, +2.9% vs Round 2, +15.1% vs pre-opt), 9.6k `5.68 s` (`1,702 tok/s`, +4.1% vs Round 2, +6.6% vs pre-opt), 15k `8.52 s` (`1,764 tok/s`, +6.0% vs pre-opt, +16.2% faster than Strata 0.2.11's 9.90 s), decode `53.1 tok/s`, bit-identical replies.** **What was built (`crates/shadow-cuda/src/kvvmm.rs`, `mmq.rs`, `session.rs`, `pinned.rs`, `crates/shadow/src/main.rs`):** (1) **VMM physical handle pool & zero-skip (`SHADOW_VMM_POOL`, default `1`):** in Round 2, `lend_kv` and `unlend_kv` still took `61-67 ms` + `29 ms` device drain in `lend_cache` and `52-65 ms` in `restore_cache` because `VmmRegion::release_tail` called `cuMemRelease` on ~650 2 MiB physical handles (`1.5 GiB`), `kv_extra_reg.map_bytes` called `cuMemCreate` ~650 times and queued a `1.5 GiB` `cuMemsetD8` (`d.zero()`) on the default stream (which `lend_cache`'s `ctx.synchronize()` then waited 29 ms for), and `unlend_kv` called `cuMemRelease` ~650 times + `cuMemCreate` ~650 times again (`~2,600` physical allocation driver calls per prompt). `VmmRegion::release_tail_to`, `map_bytes_from(..., false)`, `remap_tail_from`, and `release_pool` now move the `u64` physical handles between the KV regions and `kv_extra_reg` via `GpuSession::vmm_pool` (`cuMemUnmap` + `cuMemMap` + `cuMemSetAccess` only, zero `cuMemCreate`/`cuMemRelease` calls during the prompt) and skip zeroing scratch `kv_extra`. In `SHADOW_PHASES=1`, `lend_cache` drops from `91 ms -> 40 ms` at 3k (`55 ms -> 27 ms` at 9.6k, `45 ms -> 19 ms` at 15k) and `unlend_kv` inside `restore_cache` drops from `53 ms -> 31 ms` at 3k (`30 ms -> 18 ms` at 9.6k, `26 ms -> 16 ms` at 15k). (2) **Sub-chunk (`si > 0`) fast path & Layer-0 pipeline ordering (`SHADOW_SUBCHUNK_FAST`, default `1`):** within a layer `l`, `self.cache.map` is invariant across sub-chunks `si = 0..subs.len()`, and `si == 0` already waits on `sp.events[p]` on the default stream, so by `si >= 1` all 512 experts of layer `l` are in VRAM at `dp_eaddr`; `dev_plan_buckets` now skips rebuilding/uploading `eaddr` and `copied` on `si > 0` and returns `any_res = false` (running a single unmasked `fused_experts` phase without `stream_wait_event`), and at `l == 0` (`first_of_pass`), `moe_staged` defers `early` (`stage_copy(1)` + `stage_pipe_read(2)`) until after sub-chunk 0's `moe_combine_qwen_rows` so the GPU executes Layer 0 sub-chunk 0 while `stage_pipe_read(1)` finishes on the CPU read pool. (3) **Deduplicated `shadow_mmq_quantize` on `bb.u` (`SHADOW_MMQ_REUSE`, default `1`):** `Mmq::ds_layout(gty)` (`1` for `Q4_K | Q5_K`, `0` for `Q6_K | IQ4_XS`) + `GpuSession::mmq_y_tag` (`Cell<(u64, usize, usize, usize, u8)>`, invalidated in `hc_read_batch`, `ple_batch`, `big_batch_on`, `big_batch_off`, or when `y == x`) reuses the quantized `Q8_1` activation buffer `mmq_y` across back-to-back projections on `bb.u` (`ffn_gate_shexp` + `ffn_up_shexp` on all 48 layers, `attn_qkv` + `attn_gate` on 36 DeltaNet layers, `attn_q` + `attn_k` + `attn_v` on 12 attention layers), eliminating `~576-768` `shadow_mmq_quantize` launches per 15k prompt. (4) **Overlapped cold file reads with RAM-tier DMA in `restore_cache` (`SHADOW_RESTORE_OVERLAP`, default `1`):** queues the RAM-tier `upload_batch` (`~3.2 GiB`) onto `copy_stream` *before* reading cold file-only evicted experts into `sp.bufs[0]`, overlapping the CPU/NVMe read with the PCIe transfer (`restore_cache` drops from `205 -> 166 ms` at 3k, `180 -> 154 ms` at 9.6k, `170 -> 151 ms` at 15k). (5) **Direct mapped table reads in decode (`SHADOW_DIRECT_TAB`, default `1`):** `stage_slice` writes the per-layer routing table into `self.stage` and passes `self.stage.dev_ptr + at` directly to `mv_batch` and `moe_combine_qwen_perm` in `routed_overlapped` and `routed_qwen`, saving 48 `copy_words` launches per token step. (6) **Warning-only RAM floor check in `PinnedRegion::build` (`crates/shadow-cuda/src/pinned.rs`):** `PinnedRegion::new` and `PinnedRegion::new_chunked` now log `RAM warning:` instead of returning `Err("refusing to pin ...")` when `available_ram() < len + ram_floor()`, letting the chunked-tier watchdog (`pinned::start_watchdog`) trim 1 GiB chunks under pressure (keeping `with_ram_source` strict for unit tests). **Measured (interleaved A-B-B-A on fresh servers, `--ctx 24576 --ram-tier-gib 45 --ram-floor-gib 3.5`, `bench/prompt-opt-2026-11-10/results_r3.json`, bit-identical hashes across all 5 workloads):** on `r3_r1`'s very first prompt (`3k_a`), `available_ram` started at `48.21 GiB` (`3.40 GiB` after pinning 45 GiB), so the watchdog trimmed 2 GiB (`45 -> 43 GiB`) *before* `3k_a` on `r3_r1` (`3.97 s`) whereas on `r2_r1`, `r2_r2`, and `r3_r2` it trimmed 2 GiB *after* `3k_a`. Comparing matched-tier runs (`r3_r2` vs `r2_r2` / `r2_r1`) and two-run means: `3k_a` (2,867 tok): `r3_r2` **2.992 s (958.2 tok/s)** vs `r2_r2` 3.079 s (931.1 tok/s, **+2.9%**, and **+15.1%** vs pre-opt 3.52 s), hash `b8b75e04`; `3k_b` (2,622 tok, at 43 GiB tier): `r3` mean **3.14 s (840.0 tok/s, `r3_r2` 2.926 s / 896.2 tok/s)** vs `r2` mean 3.29 s (802.3 tok/s, `r2_r2` 3.047 s / 860.4 tok/s, **+4.2%**, and **+23.4%** vs pre-opt 3.82 s at 43 GiB tier), hash `4ee10896`; `9k6` (9,673 tok): `r3` 5.74, 5.68 s (mean **5.71 s, 1,693.7 tok/s**, `r3_r2` **1,701.6 tok/s**) vs `r2` 6.17, 5.74 s (mean **5.95 s, 1,627.2 tok/s, +4.1%**, and **+6.6%** vs pre-opt 6.09 s), hash `9034540f`; `15k` (15,034 tok): `r3` 8.55, 8.52 s (mean **8.54 s, 1,761.0 tok/s**, `r3_r2` **8.524 s / 1,763.8 tok/s**) vs `r2` 8.59, 8.61 s (mean **8.60 s, 1,748.4 tok/s, +0.7%** over Round 2, **+6.0%** vs pre-opt 9.05 s, and **+16.2% faster than Strata 0.2.11's 9.90 s / 1,543 tok/s**), hash `f095db85`; `decode200` (200 tok): `r3` **52.9 tok/s** (`53.1 tok/s` warm) vs `r2` **52.8 tok/s**, hash `46597785`. All five Round 3 switches stay on by default.
- **2026-11-10 (95): Web UI & Tauri desktop shell UX modernization (`0.1.36`, `crates/shadow-serve/src/ui.rs`, `crates/shadow-ui/ui/index.html`), and release rebuild of `shadow.exe` + `shadow-ui.exe`.** (1) **Zero-jitter live telemetry (`font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums`):** applied across `.stats-bar`, `.pill`, `.big`, `.kv b`, `table.recent`, `.msg-meta`, `.reading-note`, `#timing-info`, `#notes-hint`, and `shadow-ui/ui/index.html` so 1 Hz `/v1/telemetry` updates never shift digits horizontally. (2) **Modern form controls & IME safety:** added CSS `field-sizing: content` (with `width: 100%`, `height: auto`, `min-block-size`, `max-block-size`, and a `CSS.supports('field-sizing', 'content')` JS fallback) on `#prompt-input`, `#notes-text`, and `#set-mem-kind`; added IME-safe Enter handling (`!e.isComposing && e.keyCode !== 229`) on `#prompt-input`, `#set-mem-new`, and `.mem-edit`; added `aria-label` on all unlabelled inputs/textareas. (3) **Single-line overflow & focus rings:** added `min-inline-size: 0` + `overflow: hidden` + `text-overflow: ellipsis` on Dashboard `.kv > span` / `.kv > b`, `table-layout: fixed` on `table.recent`, and `:focus-visible` outlines (with `outline-offset: -2px` on `.chat-item` inside `#chat-list`). (4) **Chat ergonomics & persona fix:** added a floating `#scroll-bottom-btn` (`↓ Jump to latest`) when scrolled up in a conversation, `Copied ✓` feedback on code block copy buttons, global keyboard shortcuts (`Ctrl+K` / `Ctrl+/` to search conversations, `Ctrl+Shift+O` for new chat, `Esc` to close `#persona-modal` / `#thinking-dropdown` before halting generation), keyboard shortcut hints on `#welcome`, and updated `DEFAULT_PERSONA` from `by meaning` (noted in entry 91) to `by keyword, ranked by BM25 and path match` (with automatic migration of unmodified `shadow_persona` in `localStorage`).
- **2026-11-10 (96): a server-only Start menu entry: `shadow-ui.exe --headless` (`Shadow (server only)`).** The user wants to run another harness (Hermes, pi) against Shadow without the chat window. `crates/shadow-ui/src/main.rs`: with `--headless` (or `--server-only`) no webview window is built; everything else is the normal launcher - same `desktop.json` (model, port 8081), same server start, same tray icon (id `shadow`) whose tooltip now says starting / loading the model (N s) / ready / failed, and whose menu's first item reads *Open Chat in Browser* (the tray's left click and a second launch open `cfg.url` in the default browser when there is no window). Quit stops the server only if this launcher started it (unchanged). The window-less launcher stays alive with no window because Tauri only exits on a window close or an explicit exit. `tools/make_headless_shortcut.ps1` creates `%APPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Shadow (server only).lnk` -> `target\release\shadow-ui.exe --headless` (icon copied from the `Shadow` entry). **Checked:** built in `target-dev`; launched with a private config (port 8095, the model loaded by a launcher script under `tools/run_capped.ps1`): one `shadow-ui` process whose only window is the plugin's hidden single-instance window, the server came up, `/health` `{status: ok}`, `/v1/models` listed the model; stopped by killing that launcher and `POST /shutdown` on 8095 only (the user's 8081 untouched; nothing was running on it). `target\release` rebuilt (no `shadow`/`shadow-ui` was running) so the Start menu entry has the flag. **Not checked:** the tray menu itself was not clicked (Quit / Open Chat / Restart in headless mode are unchanged code paths or one-line changes, reviewed not exercised); the tooltip text was not read off the tray; a chat completion's text was not looked at (the request returned nothing readable in my shell, so only the health and model list count); starting the headless entry while the normal one is open does nothing visible (the single-instance plugin hands over to the open one and in a window-less case opens the browser).
- **2026-11-11 (97): Shadow against Strata, measured fresh for a public comparison (`docs/COMPARISON_STRATA_2026-11-11.md`, `bench/vs-strata-final-2026-11-11`).** **Setup.** Decode only (the user's choice), against the Strata build already on this PC (`build-0145/strata.exe`, 2026-10-17, the user's tuned config: 36 GiB budget, expert profile, MTP drafter, `--spec 4`; `tools/strata_bench_cfg.py` switches off the outward-facing parts, context 8,192, greedy), not the current release (0.2.11 / engine 0.1.57+; downloading it was offered and declined). `shadow-bench`, 36 varied prompts, 96 tokens, 3 interleaved runs per arm, a fresh server each, total throughput. **Result (decode tok/s).** Shadow default (`--ctx 24576`) **55.72** (55.6, 55.7, 55.9) / Strata **49.52** (50.0, 49.1, 49.6) / Shadow `--ctx 8192` **46.43** (46.3, 46.4, 46.6): **+12.5% at Shadow's default, -6.2% with the context matched to the Strata run.** Floor (run-to-run, median): Shadow 0.4%, Strata 5.3%. Per turn: wall clock 1.57 s (Shadow default) / 1.87 s (Shadow 8k) / 2.03 s (Strata), but the text per turn differs (49.9 / 56.0 / 42.0 tokens); **prompt time a turn mean 658 / 648 / 702 ms, median 618 / 588 / 411 ms: Strata answers these short prompts sooner at the median.** Ready after 38 / 38 / 33 s; lowest free RAM 5.8 / 5.2 / 9.6 GiB; peak VRAM 11.3 / 11.3 / 11.2 GiB. **How it went wrong first (all kept in `bench/`).** (1) `vs-strata-2026-11-11-attempt1-strata-failed`: the Strata server died at start (`module 'regex' has no attribute 'compile'`): the Python `regex` package in the user's site-packages had been emptied on 2026-11-06 (only a `__pycache__` left); with the user's OK `pip install --user regex` (2026.11.9) restored it. Shadow alone read 46.2 there. (2) `vs-strata-2026-11-11`: the first complete run, Shadow `--ctx 8192` 45.6 / Strata 49.1 - the reverse of the published 52.1 / 47.7. (3) `shadow-regress-2026-11-11`: to see whether Shadow had slowed, today's build against the 2026-11-10 10:24 build and the 2026-11-08 `shadow-base` build: **55.5 / 55.7 / 52.4** (2 runs each): no regression between builds. But the same exe had just read 46: my `Start-Process -ArgumentList` quoting split the arm flags, so these arms ran `serve MODEL --port 8091 --ctx` with no value = the default context 24,576 (and the default placement), while the Strata bench ran `--ctx 8192`. So **decode speed depends on `--ctx`: 55.7 at 24,576, 46.4 at 8,192, 20% apart, with the 8k run having the larger VRAM expert cache (2,317 slots against 1,843)**. The final bench states both. **What the 52.1 / 47.7 figure was.** STATUS entry 'Shadow ahead of Strata (52.1 against 47.7)' of 2026-10-31 and its repeat in the top paragraph on 2026-11-10 were taken at `--ctx 8192` and not re-measured on v0.1.36; that figure is superseded (the old text is kept, the top paragraph now points here). **Not measured / open:** (a) why `--ctx 8192` decodes 20% slower than 24,576 (a bigger cache, slower decode: unexplained; a lead for the next experiment, and a possible free +20% for anyone running Shadow with a small context); (b) prompt (prefill) speed was not run (the 3k / 9.6k / 15k figures of STATUS 65 / 93 / 94 stay as they are, from different days); (c) Strata is the older local build, not the current release; (d) the engines generate different text (Shadow 1,746 tokens a run at default, 1,469 for Strata), so decode tok/s is on different text; (e) `tools/plot_bench.py` could not run (no `PIL`; the user's Python lacks Pillow), so there are no charts or `resources.md`, only the hand-drawn `docs/comparison-2026-11-11.svg` and the per-second CSVs; (f) one machine, one model, one prompt set. Side effects on the PC: `pip install --user regex` (above); `target\release` rebuilt (`shadow.exe`, `shadow-bench.exe`, `shadow-ui.exe`) with nothing running.
- **2026-11-11 (98): the `--ctx 8192` slowdown of STATUS 97 is not about the context: after the server restores a conversation snapshot, the DFlash drafter stays off for the rest of the session (a bug; found, not fixed).** **Chase.** STATUS 97 left 'Shadow decodes 20% slower at `--ctx 8192` than at 24,576' unexplained. `bench/ctx-sweep-2026-11-11` (`--ctx` 8192 / 10240 / 12288 / 24576, 2 runs each, 36 varied prompts): 46.2, 46.4 / 46.4, 46.7 / **55.1, 40.7** / 55.7, 55.3 tok/s. Two runs of the *same* `--ctx 12288` differ by 35%, with the same expert cache (2,200 slots) and the same VRAM (peak 11,432 MiB, 795 MiB free: not oversubscribed), so it is not the context and not VRAM. The server log has the time: in the slow runs the 'speculation: N passes ... tokens in X s (drafting, checking, rolling back)' line leaves 0.9-1.0 s a request unaccounted for (~30 ms a pass) from one request on and for good; **that request is the one after `restored a conversation snapshot`**. All 20 runs of the day with a server log (sweep 8, final bench 6, regress 6, in `bench/`): every run with a restore is slow from the next request (`--ctx 8192`: restores after requests 20 and 34, slow from 20; `--ctx 10240`: 20; `--ctx 12288` run 1: 10), every run without one is not (12288 run 0, all 24576, the regress arms, the default arm of the final bench). **Mechanism (read from the code and the log, not yet proven by a fix).** `engine.rs` (3 places: the shared-prefix snapshot, a snapshot from disk, one from memory) calls `sess.dflash_break()` after `sess.restore(..)`; that sets `df.broken`, and `dflash_draft` returns no draft while it is set (`session_dflash.rs`). `broken` is cleared only in `dflash_observe` when a pass starts at position 0 (`if p0 == 0`, after the long-pass skip of `DF_ROWS`; a first prompt longer than the feature window starts at `p0 = skip`, so it does not clear it either). A chat that goes on from a restored point never reads from position 0 again. After the restore the log shows `drafting 0.00 s`, passes drafted only by lookup (`6 passes (6 drafted by lookup), 53 tokens in 1.4 s`), and most tokens made by plain one-token steps. So: **one restore turns the drafter off for the rest of the server's session**, and decode falls from ~55 to ~46 tok/s on this bench (-17%). **Why `--ctx 8192` and not 24,576.** It does not: the restore is triggered by the *text* (turn 19's reply and the next prompt share fewer tokens than the session holds, so the engine restores the snapshot taken before it); the 8,192 and 10,240 arms produce the same replies and restore at 20 and 34, the 24,576 arms' replies did not, and 12,288 flipped between its two runs. The 20% 'context effect' of STATUS 97 and the 'cache bigger, decode slower' puzzle are both this. **Fix idea (not made):** `dflash_break` is right when the snapshot is another conversation's (the drafter's K/V ring holds that conversation's positions) but wasteful when the session's own tokens start with the snapshot's: then the ring is still valid for the positions below the snapshot, so keep `broken` off and set `df.valid = min(df.valid, snapshot length)`, provided the ring still covers the window below that point (the old session ran at most RING positions past it); and let `dflash_observe` clear `broken` when a pass starts at `p0 <= skip`, not only at 0. It touches the speculation path: it needs `tools/long_agree.py` and `needle_test` agreement and an A-B on this bench before it goes in, and the replies must not change. **Effect on STATUS 97 and the comparison:** Shadow's default (55.72) is the run without a restore; the `--ctx 8192` row (46.43) is the restore bug, not a context cost; `docs/COMPARISON_STRATA_2026-11-11.md`, its chart and the README note now say so. The 2026-10-31 '52.1 against 47.7' was taken at `--ctx 8192` on an older build: whether the restore existed then is not known. **Not measured:** the fix; how often a normal chat triggers a restore (it needs only a reply that does not re-tokenize into the same tokens in the next prompt); whether the restore also costs speed through anything else (the drafter is the only thing the log shows).
